4	use log::{debug, info};
5	
6	/// What action the symlink operation needs to take.
7	#[derive(Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
8	pub enum SymlinkAction {
9	    /// Nothing exists at the symlink path — create it.
10	    Create,
11	    /// An existing symlink points somewhere else — caller must decide whether to replace.
12	    Replace { current_target: PathBuf },
13	    /// The symlink already points to the correct target — nothing to do.
14	    AlreadyCorrect,
15	}
16	
17	/// A planned symlink operation.
18	#[derive(Debug)]
19	pub struct SymLink {
20	    /// Absolute path to the target directory (what the symlink points to).
21	    pub abs_target: PathBuf,
22	    /// Full path of the symlink to create/replace.
23	    pub symlink_path: PathBuf,
24	    /// What action is needed.
25	    pub action: SymlinkAction,
26	}
27	
28	/// Encode a path the way Claude Code does: replace `/` and `.` with `-`.
29	pub fn encode_path(path: &str) -> String {
30	    path.replace(['/', '.'], "-")
31	}
32	
33	/// Read what exists at a path without following symlinks.
34	///
35	/// Returns:
36	/// - `None` — nothing exists
37	/// - `Some(None)` — exists but is not a symlink
38	/// - `Some(Some(target))` — symlink pointing to target
39	fn probe(path: &Path) -> Option<Option<PathBuf>> {
40	    match path.symlink_metadata() {
41	        Err(_) => None,
42	        Ok(meta) => {
43	            if meta.is_symlink() {
44	                Some(Some(std::fs::read_link(path).unwrap_or_default())) // OK: TOCTOU race after symlink_metadata; empty PathBuf forces recreate
45	            } else {
46	                Some(None)
47	            }
48	        }
49	    }
50	}
51	
52	impl SymLink {
53	    /// Plan a symlink operation by probing the filesystem.
54	    ///
55	    /// # Arguments
56	    /// * `cwd` — current working directory (used to derive the symlink name)
57	    /// * `target` — the directory the symlink should point to (resolved to absolute)
58	    /// * `symlink_dir` — parent directory for the symlink (e.g. `~/.claude/projects`)
59	    pub fn new(cwd: &Path, target: &Path, symlink_dir: &Path) -> Result<Self, String> {
60	        let abs_target = if target.is_absolute() {
61	            target.to_path_buf()
62	        } else {
63	            cwd.join(target)
64	        };
65	
66	        let cwd_str = cwd
67	            .to_str()
68	            .ok_or_else(|| "current directory path is not valid UTF-8".to_string())?;
69	        let symlink_name = encode_path(cwd_str);
70	        let symlink_path = symlink_dir.join(symlink_name);
71	
72	        let action = match probe(&symlink_path) {
73	            None => SymlinkAction::Create,
74	            Some(None) => {
75	                return Err(format!(
76	                    "'{}' exists and is not a symlink",
77	                    symlink_path.display()
78	                ));
79	            }
80	            Some(Some(current_target)) => {
81	                if current_target == abs_target {
82	                    SymlinkAction::AlreadyCorrect
83	                } else {
84	                    SymlinkAction::Replace { current_target }
85	                }
86	            }
87	        };
88	
89	        Ok(SymLink {
90	            abs_target,
91	            symlink_path,
92	            action,
93	        })
94	    }
95	
96	    /// Plan a symlink operation from pre-probed metadata (for testing).
97	    #[cfg(test)]
98	    pub fn with_meta(
99	        cwd: &Path,
100	        target: &Path,
101	        symlink_dir: &Path,
102	        symlink_meta: Option<Option<PathBuf>>,
103	    ) -> Result<Self, String> {
104	        let abs_target = if target.is_absolute() {
105	            target.to_path_buf()
106	        } else {
107	            cwd.join(target)
108	        };
109	
110	        let cwd_str = cwd
111	            .to_str()
112	            .ok_or_else(|| "current directory path is not valid UTF-8".to_string())?;
113	        let symlink_name = encode_path(cwd_str);
114	        let symlink_path = symlink_dir.join(symlink_name);
115	
116	        let action = match symlink_meta {
117	            None => SymlinkAction::Create,
118	            Some(None) => {
119	                return Err(format!(
120	                    "'{}' exists and is not a symlink",
121	                    symlink_path.display()
122	                ));
123	            }
124	            Some(Some(current_target)) => {
125	                if current_target == abs_target {
126	                    SymlinkAction::AlreadyCorrect
127	                } else {
128	                    SymlinkAction::Replace { current_target }
129	                }
130	            }
131	        };
132	
133	        Ok(SymLink {
134	            abs_target,
135	            symlink_path,
136	            action,
137	        })
138	    }
139	
140	    /// Execute the symlink operation: create the target dir if needed, create/replace the symlink.
141	    pub fn create(&self, create_target: bool) -> Result<(), String> {
142	        debug!(
143	            "symlink {} -> {}",
144	            self.symlink_path.display(),
145	            self.abs_target.display()
146	        );
147	
148	        if create_target && !self.abs_target.exists() {
149	            std::fs::create_dir_all(&self.abs_target).map_err(|e| {
150	                format!("cannot create target '{}': {e}", self.abs_target.display())
151	            })?;
152	        }
153	
154	        if let Some(parent) = self.symlink_path.parent() {
155	            std::fs::create_dir_all(parent).map_err(|e| {
156	                format!(
157	                    "cannot create symlink directory '{}': {e}",
158	                    parent.display()
159	                )
160	            })?;
161	        }
162	
163	        match &self.action {
164	            SymlinkAction::Create => {}
165	            SymlinkAction::Replace { .. } => {
166	                std::fs::remove_file(&self.symlink_path).map_err(|e| {
167	                    format!(
168	                        "cannot remove existing symlink '{}': {e}",
169	                        self.symlink_path.display()
170	                    )
171	                })?;
172	            }
173	            SymlinkAction::AlreadyCorrect => return Ok(()),
174	        }
175	
176	        #[cfg(unix)]
177	        std::os::unix::fs::symlink(&self.abs_target, &self.symlink_path).map_err(|e| {
178	            format!(
179	                "cannot create symlink '{}' -> '{}': {e}",
180	                self.symlink_path.display(),
181	                self.abs_target.display()
182	            )
183	        })?;
184	
185	        #[cfg(not(unix))]
186	        return Err("symlink creation is only supported on Unix".to_string());
187	
188	        Ok(())
189	    }
190	}
191	
192	// -- Subcommand --
193	
194	#[derive(Args, Debug)]
195	pub struct SymlinkArgs {
196	    /// Directory to link to [default: .claude]
197	    #[arg(value_name = "TARGET")]
198	    pub target: Option<String>,
199	
200	    /// Directory for symlink [default: ~/.claude/projects]
201	    #[arg(long, value_name = "PATH")]
202	    pub symlink_dir: Option<PathBuf>,
203	
204	    /// List contents of symlinked directory after creation
205	    #[arg(short, long)]
206	    pub list: bool,
207	
208	    /// Replace existing symlink without prompting
209	    #[arg(short, long)]
210	    pub yes: bool,
211	}
212	
213	pub fn symlink(args: &SymlinkArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
214	    debug!("symlink: enter");
215	    let cwd = std::env::current_dir()?;
216	
217	    let target = match &args.target {
218	        Some(t) => PathBuf::from(t),
219	        None => PathBuf::from(".claude"),
220	    };
221	
222	    let symlink_dir = match &args.symlink_dir {
223	        Some(d) => d.clone(),
224	        None => {
225	            let home = std::env::var("HOME")
226	                .map_err(|_| "HOME environment variable not set".to_string())?;
227	            PathBuf::from(home).join(".claude").join("projects")
228	        }
229	    };
230	
231	    let sl = SymLink::new(&cwd, &target, &symlink_dir)?;
232	
233	    // Handle interactive prompt for replacement
234	    if let SymlinkAction::Replace { ref current_target } = sl.action {
235	        info!(
236	            "Existing symlink: {} -> {}",
237	            sl.symlink_path.display(),
238	            current_target.display()
239	        );
240	        if !args.yes {
241	            let response = crate::common::prompt("Replace with new target? [y/N] ")?;
242	            if !response.eq_ignore_ascii_case("y") {
243	                return Err("aborted".into());
244	            }
245	        }
246	    }
247	
248	    if sl.action == SymlinkAction::AlreadyCorrect {
249	        info!(
250	            "Already correct: {} -> {}",
251	            sl.symlink_path.display(),
252	            sl.abs_target.display()
253	        );
254	    } else {
255	        sl.create(true)?;
256	        info!(
257	            "Created: {} -> {}",
258	            sl.symlink_path.display(),
259	            sl.abs_target.display()
260	        );
261	    }
262	
263	    if args.list {
264	        let abs_target = if target.is_absolute() {
265	            target
266	        } else {
267	            cwd.join(&target)
268	        };
269	        info!("");
270	        info!("Contents of {}:", abs_target.display());
271	        for entry in std::fs::read_dir(&abs_target)? {
272	            let entry = entry?;
273	            info!("  {}", entry.file_name().to_string_lossy());
274	        }
275	    }
276	
277	    debug!("symlink: exit");
278	    Ok(())
279	}
280	
281	#[cfg(test)]
282	mod tests {
283	    use super::*;
284	
285	    #[test]
286	    fn encode_path_basic() {
287	        assert_eq!(
288	            encode_path("/home/wink/data/prgs/vc-x1"),
289	            "-home-wink-data-prgs-vc-x1"
290	        );
291	    }
292	
293	    #[test]
294	    fn encode_path_with_dots() {
295	        assert_eq!(
296	            encode_path("/home/wink/.config/test"),
297	            "-home-wink--config-test"
298	        );
299	    }
300	
301	    #[test]
302	    fn new_create_when_nothing_exists() {
303	        let cwd = Path::new("/home/user/project");
304	        let target = Path::new(".claude");
305	        let symlink_dir = Path::new("/home/user/.claude/projects");
306	
307	        let sl = SymLink::with_meta(cwd, target, symlink_dir, None).unwrap();
308	
309	        assert_eq!(sl.abs_target, PathBuf::from("/home/user/project/.claude"));
310	        assert_eq!(
311	            sl.symlink_path,
312	            PathBuf::from("/home/user/.claude/projects/-home-user-project")
313	        );
314	        assert_eq!(sl.action, SymlinkAction::Create);
315	    }
316	
317	    #[test]
318	    fn new_already_correct() {
319	        let cwd = Path::new("/home/user/project");
320	        let target = Path::new(".claude");
321	        let symlink_dir = Path::new("/home/user/.claude/projects");
322	        let current = PathBuf::from("/home/user/project/.claude");
323	
324	        let sl = SymLink::with_meta(cwd, target, symlink_dir, Some(Some(current))).unwrap();
325	        assert_eq!(sl.action, SymlinkAction::AlreadyCorrect);
326	    }
327	
328	    #[test]
329	    fn new_replace_different_target() {
330	        let cwd = Path::new("/home/user/project");
331	        let target = Path::new(".claude");
332	        let symlink_dir = Path::new("/home/user/.claude/projects");
333	        let current = PathBuf::from("/home/user/other/.claude");
334	
335	        let sl = SymLink::with_meta(cwd, target, symlink_dir, Some(Some(current.clone()))).unwrap();
336	        assert_eq!(
337	            sl.action,
338	            SymlinkAction::Replace {
339	                current_target: current
340	            }
341	        );
342	    }
343	
344	    #[test]
345	    fn new_error_not_a_symlink() {
346	        let cwd = Path::new("/home/user/project");
347	        let target = Path::new(".claude");
348	        let symlink_dir = Path::new("/home/user/.claude/projects");
349	
350	        let err = SymLink::with_meta(cwd, target, symlink_dir, Some(None)).unwrap_err();
351	        assert!(err.contains("not a symlink"));
352	    }
353	
354	    #[test]
355	    fn new_absolute_target() {
356	        let cwd = Path::new("/home/user/project");
357	        let target = Path::new("/tmp/my-claude");
358	        let symlink_dir = Path::new("/home/user/.claude/projects");
359	
360	        let sl = SymLink::with_meta(cwd, target, symlink_dir, None).unwrap();
361	        assert_eq!(sl.abs_target, PathBuf::from("/tmp/my-claude"));
362	    }
363	
364	    #[test]
365	    fn probe_nothing() {
366	        let path = Path::new("/nonexistent/path/that/does/not/exist");
367	        assert!(probe(path).is_none());
368	    }
369	
370	    #[test]
371	    fn probe_regular_file() {
372	        let dir = std::env::temp_dir().join("symlink_test_probe_file");
373	        std::fs::create_dir_all(&dir).unwrap();
374	        let file = dir.join("regular");
375	        std::fs::write(&file, "hello").unwrap();
376	
377	        let result = probe(&file);
378	        assert_eq!(result, Some(None)); // exists, not a symlink
379	
380	        std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir).ok();
381	    }
382	
383	    #[cfg(unix)]
384	    #[test]
385	    fn probe_existing_symlink() {
386	        let dir = std::env::temp_dir().join("symlink_test_probe_link");
387	        std::fs::create_dir_all(&dir).unwrap();
388	        let target = dir.join("target_dir");
389	        std::fs::create_dir_all(&target).unwrap();
390	        let link = dir.join("the_link");
391	        std::os::unix::fs::symlink(&target, &link).unwrap();
392	
393	        let result = probe(&link);
394	        assert_eq!(result, Some(Some(target.clone())));
395	
396	        std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir).ok();
397	    }
398	
399	    #[cfg(unix)]
400	    #[test]
401	    fn create_symlink() {
402	        let dir = std::env::temp_dir().join("symlink_test_create");
403	        let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
404	        std::fs::create_dir_all(&dir).unwrap();
405	        let target = dir.join("my_target");
406	        let symlink_path = dir.join("my_link");
407	
408	        let sl = SymLink {
409	            abs_target: target.clone(),
410	            symlink_path: symlink_path.clone(),
411	            action: SymlinkAction::Create,
412	        };
413	
414	        sl.create(true).unwrap();
415	
416	        assert!(target.exists());
417	        assert!(symlink_path.symlink_metadata().unwrap().is_symlink());
418	        assert_eq!(std::fs::read_link(&symlink_path).unwrap(), target);
419	
420	        std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir).ok();
421	    }
422	
423	    #[cfg(unix)]
424	    #[test]
425	    fn replace_symlink() {
426	        let dir = std::env::temp_dir().join("symlink_test_replace");
427	        let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
428	        std::fs::create_dir_all(&dir).unwrap();
429	        let old_target = dir.join("old_target");
430	        std::fs::create_dir_all(&old_target).unwrap();
431	        let new_target = dir.join("new_target");
432	        std::fs::create_dir_all(&new_target).unwrap();
433	        let symlink_path = dir.join("the_link");
434	        std::os::unix::fs::symlink(&old_target, &symlink_path).unwrap();
435	
436	        let sl = SymLink {
437	            abs_target: new_target.clone(),
438	            symlink_path: symlink_path.clone(),
439	            action: SymlinkAction::Replace {
440	                current_target: old_target,
441	            },
442	        };
443	
444	        sl.create(false).unwrap();
445	
446	        assert_eq!(std::fs::read_link(&symlink_path).unwrap(), new_target);
447	
448	        std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir).ok();
449	    }
450	
451	    #[cfg(unix)]
452	    #[test]
453	    fn already_correct_is_noop() {
454	        let dir = std::env::temp_dir().join("symlink_test_noop");
455	        let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
456	        std::fs::create_dir_all(&dir).unwrap();
457	        let target = dir.join("target");
458	        std::fs::create_dir_all(&target).unwrap();
459	
460	        let sl = SymLink {
461	            abs_target: target,
462	            symlink_path: dir.join("nonexistent_link"),
463	            action: SymlinkAction::AlreadyCorrect,
464	        };
465	
466	        sl.create(false).unwrap();
467	        assert!(!dir.join("nonexistent_link").exists());
468	
469	        std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir).ok();
470	    }
471	}

  "content": "use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};\n\nuse clap::Args;\nuse log::{debug, info, warn};\n\nuse crate::common::run;\n\n/// Fetch and sync both repos (`.` and `.claude`) to their remotes.\n///\n/// Default is dry-run: reports per-repo state without mutating. Pass\n/// `--no-dry-run` to act. On any failure the starting state of both\n/// repos is restored via `jj op restore`.\n#[derive(Args, Debug)]\npub struct SyncArgs {\n    /// Actually perform the sync (default: dry-run)\n    #[arg(long)]\n    pub no_dry_run: bool,\n\n    /// Bookmark to sync in each repo\n    #[arg(long, default_value = \"main\")]\n    pub bookmark: String,\n\n    /// Remote to sync against\n    #[arg(long, default_value = \"origin\")]\n    pub remote: String,\n}\n\n/// Repos synced, in order. Hardcoded for now — the user's convention.\nconst REPOS: [&str; 2] = [\".\", \".claude\"];\n\n/// Relationship between a local bookmark and its remote counterpart.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub enum State {\n    /// Local and remote point at the same commit.\n    UpToDate,\n    /// Local is a strict ancestor of remote — fast-forward possible.\n    Behind { local: String, remote: String },\n    /// Remote is a strict ancestor of local — nothing to pull in.\n    Ahead { local: String, remote: String },\n    /// Neither is an ancestor of the other — needs rebase.\n    Diverged { local: String, remote: String },\n    /// The bookmark has no `@<remote>` counterpart.\n    NoRemote,\n}\n\n/// Per-repo context accumulated before the action phase.\n#[derive(Debug)]\nstruct RepoCtx {\n    path: PathBuf,\n    op_id: String,\n    state: State,\n}\n\npub fn sync(args: &SyncArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    debug!(\"sync: enter (no_dry_run={}, bookmark={}, remote={})\",\n        args.no_dry_run, args.bookmark, args.remote);\n\n    let repos: Vec<PathBuf> = REPOS.iter().map(PathBuf::from).collect();\n\n    // Pre-flight: every repo must exist, be a jj repo, and have a clean @.\n    for repo in &repos {\n        check_clean(repo)?;\n    }\n\n    // Snapshot starting op id per repo — used to revert on any failure.\n    let mut snapshots: Vec<(PathBuf, String)> = Vec::new();\n    for repo in &repos {\n        let op_id = current_op_id(repo)?;\n        debug!(\"{}: op snapshot = {op_id}\", repo.display());\n        snapshots.push((repo.clone(), op_id));\n    }\n\n    // Fetch + classify + (optionally) act, with revert-on-failure.\n    let result = run_plan(&snapshots, args);\n\n    if let Err(e) = &result {\n        warn!(\"sync failed: {e}\");\n        warn!(\"reverting all repos to starting state...\");\n        for (repo, op_id) in &snapshots {\n            match op_restore(repo, op_id) {\n                Ok(()) => info!(\"  {}: reverted to op {op_id}\", repo.display()),\n                Err(re) => warn!(\"  {}: revert failed: {re}\", repo.display()),\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    debug!(\"sync: exit\");\n    result\n}\n\nfn run_plan(\n    snapshots: &[(PathBuf, String)],\n    args: &SyncArgs,\n) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    // Fetch and classify each repo.\n    let mut ctxs: Vec<RepoCtx> = Vec::new();\n    for (repo, op_id) in snapshots {\n        info!(\"{}: fetch {}\", repo.display(), args.remote);\n        run(\n            \"jj\",\n            &[\"git\", \"fetch\", \"--remote\", &args.remote, \"-R\", &repo_str(repo)],\n            Path::new(\".\"),\n        )?;\n        let state = classify(repo, &args.bookmark, &args.remote)?;\n        log_state(repo, &state);\n        ctxs.push(RepoCtx {\n            path: repo.clone(),\n            op_id: op_id.clone(),\n            state,\n        });\n    }\n\n    // Act, unless dry-run. Any error returns and triggers the outer revert.\n    for ctx in &ctxs {\n        act_on_state(ctx, args)?;\n    }\n\n    if !args.no_dry_run {\n        info!(\"dry-run — re-run with --no-dry-run to apply\");\n    }\n    Ok(())\n}\n\nfn act_on_state(ctx: &RepoCtx, args: &SyncArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let repo = &ctx.path;\n    let remote_rev = format!(\"{}@{}\", args.bookmark, args.remote);\n    match &ctx.state {\n        State::UpToDate | State::Ahead { .. } | State::NoRemote => Ok(()),\n        State::Behind { .. } => {\n            if args.no_dry_run {\n                info!(\"{}: fast-forwarding '{}'\", repo.display(), args.bookmark);\n                run(\n                    \"jj\",\n                    &[\n                        \"bookmark\", \"set\", &args.bookmark, \"-r\", &remote_rev,\n                        \"-R\", &repo_str(repo),\n                    ],\n                    Path::new(\".\"),\n                )?;\n            }\n            Ok(())\n        }\n        State::Diverged { .. } => {\n            if args.no_dry_run {\n                info!(\"{}: rebasing '{}' onto {}\", repo.display(), args.bookmark, remote_rev);\n                run(\n                    \"jj\",\n                    &[\n                        \"rebase\", \"-b\", &args.bookmark, \"-d\", &remote_rev,\n                        \"-R\", &repo_str(repo),\n                    ],\n                    Path::new(\".\"),\n                )?;\n                if has_conflicts(repo)? {\n                    return Err(format!(\n                        \"{}: rebase produced conflicts\",\n                        repo.display()\n                    )\n                    .into());\n                }\n            }\n            Ok(())\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nfn log_state(repo: &Path, state: &State) {\n    let r = repo.display();\n    match state {\n        State::UpToDate => info!(\"{r}: up-to-date\"),\n        State::NoRemote => info!(\"{r}: no remote counterpart — skipping\"),\n        State::Ahead { local, remote } => {\n            info!(\"{r}: ahead (local {local} > remote {remote}); nothing to sync\")\n        }\n        State::Behind { local, remote } => {\n            info!(\"{r}: behind (local {local} < remote {remote}); would fast-forward\")\n        }\n        State::Diverged { local, remote } => {\n            info!(\"{r}: diverged (local {local} vs remote {remote}); would rebase\")\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nfn check_clean(repo: &Path) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let out = run(\"jj\", &[\"st\", \"-R\", &repo_str(repo)], Path::new(\".\"))?;\n    if out.contains(\"Working copy changes:\") {\n        return Err(format!(\n            \"{}: working copy has uncommitted changes — commit or abandon first\",\n            repo.display()\n        )\n        .into());\n    }\n    Ok(())\n}\n\nfn current_op_id(repo: &Path) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let out = run(\n        \"jj\",\n        &[\n            \"op\", \"log\", \"--no-graph\", \"-n\", \"1\",\n            \"-T\", \"id.short(12)\",\n            \"-R\", &repo_str(repo),\n        ],\n        Path::new(\".\"),\n    )?;\n    Ok(out.trim().to_string())\n}\n\nfn op_restore(repo: &Path, op_id: &str) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    run(\n        \"jj\",\n        &[\"op\", \"restore\", op_id, \"-R\", &repo_str(repo)],\n        Path::new(\".\"),\n    )?;\n    Ok(())\n}\n\nfn classify(\n    repo: &Path,\n    bookmark: &str,\n    remote: &str,\n) -> Result<State, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let local = commit_id(repo, bookmark)?;\n    let remote_rev = format!(\"{bookmark}@{remote}\");\n    let Some(remote) = try_commit_id(repo, &remote_rev)? else {\n        return Ok(State::NoRemote);\n    };\n    if local == remote {\n        return Ok(State::UpToDate);\n    }\n    let local_is_anc = revset_nonempty(repo, &format!(\"{bookmark}::{remote_rev}\"))?;\n    let remote_is_anc = revset_nonempty(repo, &format!(\"{remote_rev}::{bookmark}\"))?;\n    Ok(match (local_is_anc, remote_is_anc) {\n        (true, _) => State::Behind { local, remote },\n        (false, true) => State::Ahead { local, remote },\n        (false, false) => State::Diverged { local, remote },\n    })\n}\n\nfn commit_id(repo: &Path, rev: &str) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let out = run(\n        \"jj\",\n        &[\n            \"log\", \"-r\", rev, \"--no-graph\",\n            \"-T\", \"commit_id.short(12)\",\n            \"-R\", &repo_str(repo),\n        ],\n        Path::new(\".\"),\n    )?;\n    Ok(out.trim().to_string())\n}\n\nfn try_commit_id(\n    repo: &Path,\n    rev: &str,\n) -> Result<Option<String>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    match commit_id(repo, rev) {\n        Ok(id) if id.is_empty() => Ok(None),\n        Ok(id) => Ok(Some(id)),\n        Err(e) => {\n            // \"doesn't exist\" means the revset couldn't resolve — treat as absent.\n            let msg = e.to_string();\n            if msg.contains(\"doesn't exist\") || msg.contains(\"No such revision\") {\n                Ok(None)\n            } else {\n                Err(e)\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nfn revset_nonempty(repo: &Path, revset: &str) -> Result<bool, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let out = run(\n        \"jj\",\n        &[\n            \"log\", \"-r\", revset, \"--no-graph\",\n            \"-T\", r#\"commit_id.short() ++ \"\\n\"\"#,\n            \"-R\", &repo_str(repo),\n        ],\n        Path::new(\".\"),\n    )?;\n    Ok(!out.trim().is_empty())\n}\n\nfn has_conflicts(repo: &Path) -> Result<bool, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    revset_nonempty(repo, \"conflicts()\")\n}\n\nfn repo_str(p: &Path) -> String {\n    p.to_string_lossy().into_owned()\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_defaults() {\n        use clap::Parser;\n        #[derive(Parser)]\n        struct Cli {\n            #[command(flatten)]\n            args: SyncArgs,\n        }\n        let cli = Cli::try_parse_from([\"test\"]).unwrap();\n        assert!(!cli.args.no_dry_run);\n        assert_eq!(cli.args.bookmark, \"main\");\n        assert_eq!(cli.args.remote, \"origin\");\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_overrides() {\n        use clap::Parser;\n        #[derive(Parser)]\n        struct Cli {\n            #[command(flatten)]\n            args: SyncArgs,\n        }\n        let cli = Cli::try_parse_from([\n            \"test\",\n            \"--no-dry-run\",\n            \"--bookmark\",\n            \"dev\",\n            \"--remote\",\n            \"upstream\",\n        ])\n        .unwrap();\n        assert!(cli.args.no_dry_run);\n        assert_eq!(cli.args.bookmark, \"dev\");\n        assert_eq!(cli.args.remote, \"upstream\");\n    }\n}\n"
}

A non-empty result means the rebase produced\n///   conflicted commits; return `Err` so the outer revert restores the\n///   pre-fetch state.\nfn act_on_state(ctx: &RepoCtx, args: &SyncArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let repo = &ctx.path;\n    let remote_rev = format!(\"{}@{}\", args.bookmark, args.remote);\n    match &ctx.state {\n        State::UpToDate | args.bookmark);\n                run(\n                    \"jj\",\n                    &[\n                        \"bookmark\", \"set\", &args.bookmark, \"-r\", &remote_rev,\n info!(\"{r}: no remote counterpart — skipping\"),\n        State::Ahead { local, remote } => {\n            info!(\"{r}: ahead (local {local} > remote {remote}); nothing to sync\")\n        }\n cleanly returns to the starting state.\nfn check_clean(repo: &Path) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let out = run(\"jj\", &[\"st\", \"-R\", &repo_str(repo)], Path::new(\".\"))?;\n    if out.contains(\"Working copy changes:\") {\n        return Err(format!(\n            \"{}: working copy has uncommitted changes — commit or abandon first\",\n            repo.display()\n        )\n        .into());\n    }\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n/// Return the id of the most recent operation on `repo`.\n///\n/// Used as the revert target on failure.\nfn current_op_id(repo: &Path) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let out = run(\n        \"jj\",\n        &[\n            \"op\", \"log\", \"--no-graph\", \"-n\", \"1\",\n            \"-T\", \"id.short(12)\",\n            \"-R\", &repo_str(repo),\n        ],\n        Path::new(\".\"),\n    )?;\n    Ok(out.trim().to_string())\n}\n\n/// Restore `repo` to the operation identified by `op_id`.\n///\n/// Thin wrapper around `jj op restore`. Called during the failure\n/// revert path — drops the caller's returned stdout.\nfn op_restore(repo: &Path, op_id: &str) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    run(\n        \"jj\",\n        &[\"op\", \"restore\", op_id, \"-R\", &repo_str(repo)],\n        Path::new(\".\"),\n    )?;\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n/// Classify the relationship between `bookmark` and `bookmark@remote`.\n///\n/// Resolves both endpoints to commit ids and compares them via two\n/// revset-ancestry probes (`a::b`, `b::a`). When the remote bookmark\n/// does not resolve, returns `NoRemote` — the caller's action phase\n/// treats that as a no-op (and prints a skip line).\nfn classify(\n    repo: &Path,\n    bookmark: &str,\n    remote: &str,\n) -> Result<State, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let local = commit_id(repo, bookmark)?;\n    let remote_rev = format!(\"{bookmark}@{remote}\");\n    let Some(remote) = try_commit_id(repo, &remote_rev)? else {\n        return Ok(State::NoRemote);\n    };\n    if local == remote {\n        return Ok(State::UpToDate);\n    }\n    let local_is_anc = revset_nonempty(repo, &format!(\"{bookmark}::{remote_rev}\"))?;\n    let remote_is_anc = revset_nonempty(repo, &format!(\"{remote_rev}::{bookmark}\"))?;\n    Ok(match (local_is_anc, remote_is_anc) {\n        (true, _) => State::Behind { local, remote },\n        (false, true) => State::Ahead { local, remote },\n        (false, false) => State::Diverged { local, remote },\n    })\n}\n\n/// Return the short commit id that `rev` resolves to in `repo`.\n///\n/// Errors when the revset is unresolvable or matches multiple heads\n/// — callers that want to tolerate \"revset doesn't resolve\" should use\n/// `try_commit_id`.\nfn commit_id(repo: &Path, rev: &str) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let out = run(\n        \"jj\",\n        &[\n            \"log\", \"-r\", rev, \"--no-graph\",\n            \"-T\", \"commit_id.short(12)\",\n            \"-R\", &repo_str(repo),\n        ],\n        Path::new(\".\"),\n    )?;\n    Ok(out.trim().to_string())\n}\n\n/// Like `commit_id`, but `Ok(None)` when the revset doesn't resolve.\n///\n/// jj reports missing revisions via stderr strings like\n/// `Revision \\`foo@origin\\` doesn't exist` or `No such revision …`;\n/// both get mapped to `Ok(None)` so callers can distinguish \"missing\"\n/// from \"other subprocess failure\".\nfn try_commit_id(\n    repo: &Path,\n    rev: &str,\n) -> Result<Option<String>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    match commit_id(repo, rev) {\n        Ok(id) if id.is_empty() => Ok(None),\n        Ok(id) => Ok(Some(id)),\n        Err(e) => {\n            let msg = e.to_string();\n            if msg.contains(\"doesn't exist\") || msg.contains(\"No such revision\") {\n                Ok(None)\n            } else {\n                Err(e)\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Return `true` when `revset` matches at least one commit in `repo`.\nfn revset_nonempty(repo: &Path, revset: &str) -> Result<bool, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let out = run(\n        \"jj\",\n        &[\n            \"log\", \"-r\", revset, \"--no-graph\",\n            \"-T\", r#\"commit_id.short() ++ \"\\n\"\"#,\n            \"-R\", &repo_str(repo),\n        ],\n        Path::new(\".\"),\n    )?;\n    Ok(!out.trim().is_empty())\n}\n\n/// Return `true` when `repo` has any conflicted commits.\nfn has_conflicts(repo: &Path) -> Result<bool, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    revset_nonempty(repo, \"conflicts()\")\n}\n\n/// Convert a path to a `String` suitable for passing as a subprocess arg.\nfn repo_str(p: &Path) -> String {\n    p.to_string_lossy().into_owned()\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    /// Default flags: dry-run on, bookmark \"main\", remote \"origin\".\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_defaults() {\n        use clap::Parser;\n        #[derive(Parser)]\n        struct Cli {\n            #[command(flatten)]\n            args: SyncArgs,\n        }\n        let cli = Cli::try_parse_from([\"test\"]).unwrap();\n        assert!(!cli.args.no_dry_run);\n        assert_eq!(cli.args.bookmark, \"main\");\n        assert_eq!(cli.args.remote, \"origin\");\n    }\n\n    /// Overrides: `--no-dry-run`, `--bookmark`, `--remote` all honored.\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_overrides() {\n        use clap::Parser;\n        #[derive(Parser)]\n        struct Cli {\n            #[command(flatten)]\n            args: SyncArgs,\n        }\n        let cli = Cli::try_parse_from([\n            \"test\",\n            \"--no-dry-run\",\n            \"--bookmark\",\n            \"dev\",\n            \"--remote\",\n            \"upstream\",\n        ])\n        .unwrap();\n        assert!(cli.args.no_dry_run);\n        assert_eq!(cli.args.bookmark, \"dev\");\n        assert_eq!(cli.args.remote, \"upstream\");\n    }\n}\n"
}
```

  "content": "---\nname: Add doc comments on every fn/method\ndescription: Every function (and method) in new vc-x1 source files must have a doc comment explaining what it does. Matches existing project style.\ntype: feedback\n---\n\nEvery `fn` / method in new vc-x1 source should carry a `///` doc comment\n(even private helpers). CLAUDE.md's default \"write no comments\" is\n*overridden* for this project — the existing modules (`common.rs`,\n`symlink.rs`, `finalize.rs`, etc.) already have doc comments on\nessentially every function, including private helpers and small\nwrappers.\n\n**Why:** Wink explicitly asked for this when reviewing a new module\ndraft. Matches the house style — public API and internal helpers get\nthe same treatment, so readers don't have to infer intent from names\nalone.\n\n**How to apply:** Whenever writing or editing a function in `src/*.rs`,\ninclude a `///` doc comment. Keep it short — one line for simple\nwrappers, a short paragraph for orchestration functions explaining the\nflow / why it's structured that way. Struct fields don't need comments\nunless their meaning isn't obvious from the name.\n"
}

<system-reminder>This memory is 6 days old. Memories are point-in-time observations, not live state — claims about code behavior or file:line citations may be outdated. Verify against current code before asserting as fact.</system-reminder>
1	# Memory
2	
3	## User Preferences
4	
5	- **Commit approval**: Always present the full commit commands to the user for physical review BEFORE executing them. Do not run jj commit / git commit until the user explicitly approves.
6	- **Versioning**: Every plan must start with a version bump per notes/README.md "Versioning during development". Get user approval on single-step vs multi-step approach before starting.
7	- **Pre-commit checklist**: Before committing, run: tests, clippy, fmt, install the app, then retest.
8	
9	## Feedback
10	
11	- [Always include ochid trailer in commits](feedback_ochid_trailer.md)
12	- [Always commit .claude repo before finalize](feedback_claude_commit_before_finalize.md)
13	- [Skip .claude push before finalize — finalize handles it](feedback_no_claude_push.md)
14	- [Finalize must be absolute last action — only status line after](feedback_finalize_last.md)
15	- [Use --ignore-immutable for late squash after push](feedback_squash_ignore_immutable.md)
16	- [Use vc-x1 chid to retrieve changeIDs, not jj log](feedback_use_chid.md)
17	- [Recognize when stuck — 5+ min thinking on simple task means stuck](feedback_stuck_detection.md)
18	- [No --allow-new flag for jj git push](feedback_jj_no_allow_new.md)
19	- [No text output after finalize — trailing writes miss squash window](feedback_no_finalize_echo.md)
20	- [Ask for clarification on ambiguous input instead of spinning](feedback_ask_on_ambiguity.md)
21	- ["finalize steps" triggers full session-end workflow](feedback_finalize_steps.md)
22	- [Use relative paths in commands for brevity](feedback_relative_paths.md)
23	- [Use -L for ochid retrieval — clean parseable output](feedback_use_no_label.md)
24	- [Step-by-step approval for commit/push/finalize](feedback_step_by_step_approval.md) — no batching when something deviates
25	
26	
27	## Lessons Learned
28	
29	- **Avoid duplicate commits in .claude repo**: Committing the bot session repo multiple times in one session can create duplicate-titled commits with other session commits interleaved, making squashing non-trivial. Prefer a single commit to .claude at session end.
30	- **ochid trailers need updating after squash**: When squashing changes the target commit in .claude, the ochid reference in the app repo must be updated to point at the correct changeID.
31	- **Don't modify .claude repo programmatically mid-session**: Session data updates continuously, so bot-initiated changes to .claude can create circular modifications.
32	
33	## Key Files
34	
35	- notes/README.md — contains versioning workflow, todo format rules
36	- notes/todo.md — near-term task tracking
37	- notes/chores-01.md — chore details and reference links
38	- CLAUDE.md — dual repo commit/push workflow
39	- [User profile](user_profile.md) — Wink Saville, long-time collaborator on vc-x1 from inception
40	
```

```
Fetch and sync both repos (`.` and `.claude`) to their remotes.

Default is dry-run — re-run with --no-dry-run to apply. Per repo:
  - up-to-date        nothing to do
  - behind            fast-forward bookmark to remote
  - ahead             nothing to sync (local has unpushed work)
  - diverged          rebase local onto remote; fail on conflicts
  - no remote         bookmark has no @<remote> counterpart; skip

Pre-flight requires a clean working copy in each repo. On any
failure, both repos are reverted to their starting state via
`jj op restore`.

Usage: vc-x1 sync [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --no-dry-run
          Actually perform the sync (default: dry-run)

  -v, --verbose...
          Verbose output: -v debug, -vv trace

      --bookmark <BOOKMARK>
          Bookmark to sync in each repo
          
          [default: main]

      --log <LOG>
          Log file path (captures all levels)

      --remote <REMOTE>
          Remote to sync against
          
          [default: origin]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')
```

225	///

229	/// (via `jj op restore`) cleanly returns to the starting state.
230	fn check_clean(repo: &Path) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {

  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "/// Fail if `repo` has any working-copy modifications.\n///\n/// Detected by the literal `\"Working copy changes:\"` header in\n/// `jj st` output — present when there are modifications, absent on a\n/// clean `@`. `sync` refuses to run against a dirty repo so a revert\n/// (via `jj op restore`) cleanly returns to the starting state.\nfn check_clean(repo: &Path) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let out = run(\"jj\", &[\"st\", \"-R\", &repo_str(repo)], Path::new(\".\"))?;\n    if out.contains(\"Working copy changes:\") {\n        return Err(format!(\n            \"{}: working copy has uncommitted changes — commit or abandon first\",\n            repo.display()\n        )\n        .into());\n    }\n    Ok(())\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Fail if `repo`'s working-copy commit (`@`) is not empty.\n///\n/// \"Empty\" means no diff from its parent — no uncommitted edits, no\n/// in-progress session writes. The `.claude` repo is non-empty during\n/// an active bot session, and the app repo is non-empty when the user\n/// has uncommitted edits; in either case, sync's revert path (via\n/// `jj op restore`) would roll state back across that work, so we\n/// refuse up front.\nfn check_clean(repo: &Path) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    if !revset_nonempty(repo, \"@ & empty()\")? {\n        return Err(format!(\n            \"{}: working-copy commit (@) is not empty — commit or abandon first\",\n            repo.display()\n        )\n        .into());\n    }\n    Ok(())\n}"
}

280	/// Resolves both endpoints to commit ids and compares them via two
281	/// revset-ancestry probes (`a::b`, `b::a`). When the remote bookmark
282	/// does not resolve, returns `NoRemote` — the caller's action phase
283	/// treats that as a no-op (and prints a skip line).
284	fn classify(
285	    repo: &Path,
286	    bookmark: &str,
287	    remote: &str,
288	) -> Result<State, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
289	    let local = commit_id(repo, bookmark)?;
290	    let remote_rev = format!("{bookmark}@{remote}");
291	    let Some(remote) = try_commit_id(repo, &remote_rev)? else {
292	        return Ok(State::NoRemote);
293	    };
294	    if local == remote {
295	        return Ok(State::UpToDate);
296	    }
297	    let local_is_anc = revset_nonempty(repo, &format!("{bookmark}::{remote_rev}"))?;
298	    let remote_is_anc = revset_nonempty(repo, &format!("{remote_rev}::{bookmark}"))?;
299	    Ok(match (local_is_anc, remote_is_anc) {
300	        (true, _) => State::Behind { local, remote },
301	        (false, true) => State::Ahead { local, remote },
302	        (false, false) => State::Diverged { local, remote },
303	    })
304	}
305	
306	/// Return the short commit id that `rev` resolves to in `repo`.
307	///
308	/// Errors when the revset is unresolvable or matches multiple heads
309	/// — callers that want to tolerate "revset doesn't resolve" should use
310	/// `try_commit_id`.
311	fn commit_id(repo: &Path, rev: &str) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
312	    let out = run(
313	        "jj",
314	        &[
315	            "log",
316	            "-r",
317	            rev,
318	            "--no-graph",
319	            "-T",
320	            "commit_id.short(12)",
321	            "-R",
322	            &repo_str(repo),
323	        ],
324	        Path::new("."),
325	    )?;
326	    Ok(out.trim().to_string())
327	}
328	
329	/// Like `commit_id`, but `Ok(None)` when the revset doesn't resolve.
330	///
331	/// jj reports missing revisions via stderr strings like
332	/// `Revision \`foo@origin\` doesn't exist` or `No such revision …`;
333	/// both get mapped to `Ok(None)` so callers can distinguish "missing"
334	/// from "other subprocess failure".
335	fn try_commit_id(repo: &Path, rev: &str) -> Result<Option<String>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
336	    match commit_id(repo, rev) {
337	        Ok(id) if id.is_empty() => Ok(None),
338	        Ok(id) => Ok(Some(id)),
339	        Err(e) => {
340	            let msg = e.to_string();
341	            if msg.contains("doesn't exist") || msg.contains("No such revision") {
342	                Ok(None)
343	            } else {
344	                Err(e)
345	            }
346	        }
347	    }
348	}
349

351	fn revset_nonempty(repo: &Path, revset: &str) -> Result<bool, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
352	    let out = run(
353	        "jj",
354	        &[
355	            "log",
356	            "-r",
357	            revset,
358	            "--no-graph",
359	            "-T",

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/sync.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Classify the relationship between `bookmark` and `bookmark@remote`.\n///\n/// Resolves both endpoints to commit ids and compares them via two\n/// revset-ancestry probes (`a::b`, `b::a`). When the remote bookmark\n/// does not resolve, returns `NoRemote` — the caller's action phase\n/// treats that as a no-op (and prints a skip line).\nfn classify(\n    repo: &Path,\n    bookmark: &str,\n    remote: &str,\n) -> Result<State, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let local = commit_id(repo, bookmark)?;\n    let remote_rev = format!(\"{bookmark}@{remote}\");\n    let Some(remote) = try_commit_id(repo, &remote_rev)? else {\n        return Ok(State::NoRemote);\n    };\n    if local == remote {\n        return Ok(State::UpToDate);\n    }\n    let local_is_anc = revset_nonempty(repo, &format!(\"{bookmark}::{remote_rev}\"))?;\n    let remote_is_anc = revset_nonempty(repo, &format!(\"{remote_rev}::{bookmark}\"))?;\n    Ok(match (local_is_anc, remote_is_anc) {\n        (true, _) => State::Behind { local, remote },\n        (false, true) => State::Ahead { local, remote },\n        (false, false) => State::Diverged { local, remote },\n    })\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Classify the relationship between `bookmark` and `bookmark@remote`.\n///\n/// Uses `bookmarks(<b>)` rather than the bare name so a conflicted\n/// bookmark (jj's representation of a diverged fetch) resolves to all\n/// of its heads instead of erroring. When the set has multiple heads,\n/// the bookmark is conflicted and the repo is `Diverged` by definition.\n/// Otherwise we compare the single local head against the single\n/// remote commit via two revset-ancestry probes.\n///\n/// Returns `NoRemote` when `<b>@<remote>` does not resolve — the caller\n/// logs a skip and moves on.\nfn classify(\n    repo: &Path,\n    bookmark: &str,\n    remote: &str,\n) -> Result<State, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let local_heads = local_bookmark_heads(repo, bookmark)?;\n    let remote_rev = format!(\"{bookmark}@{remote}\");\n    let Some(remote) = try_commit_id(repo, &remote_rev)? else {\n        return Ok(State::NoRemote);\n    };\n    if local_heads.is_empty() {\n        return Err(format!(\"{}: bookmark '{bookmark}' does not exist\", repo.display()).into());\n    }\n    if local_heads.len() > 1 {\n        // Conflicted bookmark — jj's shape for post-fetch divergence.\n        let local = local_heads.join(\",\");\n        return Ok(State::Diverged { local, remote });\n    }\n    let local = local_heads.into_iter().next().unwrap(); // OK: `len() > 1` arm handled above, `is_empty()` handled above\n    if local == remote {\n        return Ok(State::UpToDate);\n    }\n    let local_is_anc = revset_nonempty(repo, &format!(\"{local}::{remote_rev}\"))?;\n    let remote_is_anc = revset_nonempty(repo, &format!(\"{remote_rev}::{local}\"))?;\n    Ok(match (local_is_anc, remote_is_anc) {\n        (true, _) => State::Behind { local, remote },\n        (false, true) => State::Ahead { local, remote },\n        (false, false) => State::Diverged { local, remote },\n    })\n}\n\n/// Return all commit ids the `bookmark` currently points at.\n///\n/// Normally a one-element vector; two or more elements indicate a\n/// conflicted bookmark (jj's representation of diverged post-fetch\n/// state, where the local bookmark has multiple heads).\nfn local_bookmark_heads(\n    repo: &Path,\n    bookmark: &str,\n) -> Result<Vec<String>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let out = run(\n        \"jj\",\n        &[\n            \"log\",\n            \"-r\",\n            &format!(\"bookmarks(exact:{bookmark})\"),\n            \"--no-graph\",\n            \"-T\",\n            r#\"commit_id.short(12) ++ \"\\n\"\"#,\n            \"-R\",\n            &repo_str(repo),\n        ],\n        Path::new(\".\"),\n    )?;\n    Ok(out\n        .lines()\n        .map(|l| l.trim().to_string())\n        .filter(|l| !l.is_empty())\n        .collect())\n}"
}

152	fn act_on_state(ctx: &RepoCtx, args: &SyncArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
153	    let repo = &ctx.path;
154	    let remote_rev = format!("{}@{}", args.bookmark, args.remote);
155	    match &ctx.state {
156	        State::UpToDate | State::Ahead { .. } | State::NoRemote => Ok(()),
157	        State::Behind { .. } => {
158	            if args.no_dry_run {
159	                info!("{}: fast-forwarding '{}'", repo.display(), args.bookmark);
160	                run(
161	                    "jj",
162	                    &[
163	                        "bookmark",
164	                        "set",
165	                        &args.bookmark,
166	                        "-r",
167	                        &remote_rev,
168	                        "-R",
169	                        &repo_str(repo),
170	                    ],
171	                    Path::new("."),
172	                )?;
173	            }
174	            Ok(())
175	        }
176	        State::Diverged { .. } => {
177	            if args.no_dry_run {
178	                info!(
179	                    "{}: rebasing '{}' onto {}",
180	                    repo.display(),
181	                    args.bookmark,
182	                    remote_rev
183	                );
184	                run(
185	                    "jj",
186	                    &[
187	                        "rebase",
188	                        "-b",
189	                        &args.bookmark,
190	                        "-d",
191	                        &remote_rev,
192	                        "-R",
193	                        &repo_str(repo),
194	                    ],
195	                    Path::new("."),
196	                )?;
197	                if has_conflicts(repo)? {
198	                    return Err(format!("{}: rebase produced conflicts", repo.display()).into());
199	                }
200	            }
201	            Ok(())
202	        }
203	    }
204	}
205	
206	/// Print a single-line summary of `state` at `info!` level.
```

  "old_string": "        State::Diverged { .. } => {\n            if args.no_dry_run {\n                info!(\n                    \"{}: rebasing '{}' onto {}\",\n                    repo.display(),\n                    args.bookmark,\n                    remote_rev\n                );\n                run(\n                    \"jj\",\n                    &[\n                        \"rebase\",\n                        \"-b\",\n                        &args.bookmark,\n                        \"-d\",\n                        &remote_rev,\n                        \"-R\",\n                        &repo_str(repo),\n                    ],\n                    Path::new(\".\"),\n                )?;\n                if has_conflicts(repo)? {\n                    return Err(format!(\"{}: rebase produced conflicts\", repo.display()).into());\n                }\n            }\n            Ok(())\n        }",
  "new_string": "        State::Diverged { local, remote } => {\n            if args.no_dry_run {\n                // `local` is either a single commit id or a comma-joined list\n                // of heads when the bookmark is conflicted. Pick the head\n                // that isn't the remote — that's the local-only tip. The\n                // comma-joined path covers the jj post-fetch divergence\n                // shape (local bookmark conflicted between old local head\n                // and freshly-fetched remote head).\n                let local_head = local\n                    .split(',')\n                    .find(|h| *h != remote)\n                    .unwrap_or(local.as_str());\n                info!(\n                    \"{}: rebasing {local_head} onto {remote_rev}\",\n                    repo.display()\n                );\n                run(\n                    \"jj\",\n                    &[\n                        \"rebase\",\n                        \"-b\",\n                        local_head,\n                        \"-d\",\n                        &remote_rev,\n                        \"-R\",\n                        &repo_str(repo),\n                    ],\n                    Path::new(\".\"),\n                )?;\n                if has_conflicts(repo)? {\n                    return Err(format!(\"{}: rebase produced conflicts\", repo.display()).into());\n                }\n            }\n            Ok(())\n        }"
}

```
354	### symlink
355	
356	Create or verify the Claude Code project symlink. Claude Code stores
357	session data in `~/.claude/projects/<encoded-path>/`. This command
358	creates a symlink from that location to the local `.claude` directory.
359	
360	```
361	# Create symlink for current project (default target: .claude)
362	vc-x1 symlink
363	
364	# Specify a different target
365	vc-x1 symlink /path/to/session-dir
366	
367	# Replace existing symlink without prompting
368	vc-x1 symlink -y
369	
370	# List contents after creation
371	vc-x1 symlink -l
372	```
373	
374	| Flag | Description |
375	|------|-------------|
376	| `--symlink-dir <PATH>` | Override symlink parent [default: ~/.claude/projects] |
377	| `-l, --list` | List contents of symlinked directory after creation |
378	| `-y, --yes` | Replace existing symlink without prompting |
379	
380	### finalize
381	
382	Atomically squash, set bookmark, and push a jj repo. The primary use
383	case is the bot finalizing its own session repo (`.claude`) at the end
384	of a session. The bot can't just `jj commit` because the act of
385	committing generates more session data — files written after the commit
386	would be lost. `finalize` solves this by:
387	
388	1. **`--detach`**: spawning a background process so the bot session can
389	   end immediately (no more writes after the bot exits)
390	2. **`--delay`**: waiting for trailing writes to settle
391	3. **`--squash`**: squashing the working copy into the session commit
392	4. **`--bookmark`** + **`--push`**: advancing the bookmark and pushing
393	
394	Every behavior is opt-in — omit any flag to skip that step.
395	
396	The bot's last action in a session:
397	
398	```
399	vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --bookmark main --delay 10 --detach --push
400	```
401	
402	If there is non-written session data after a session ends (e.g.
403	finalize failed or was skipped), run it manually:
404	
405	```
406	vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --bookmark main --push
407	```
408	
409	This runs in the foreground, squashes, advances the bookmark, and pushes.
410	
411	Other uses — `finalize` composes freely:
412	
413	```
414	# Just set bookmark, no squash
415	vc-x1 finalize --bookmark main
416	
417	# Squash with custom source/target
418	vc-x1 finalize --squash @,@-- --bookmark main
419	```
420	
421	See [finalize subcommand](./notes/chores-01.md#finalize-subcommand-for-session-repo-coherence)
422	for design details.
423	
```

```
30	changeID in the commit message to repo B. Thus there is a cross reference
31	between the two repos and this will allow vc-x1 to show how the repo
32	evolved and the entity (bot or human) can more clearly understand **how** and
33	most importantly **why** the code evolved.
34	
35	The solution space is wide open, from trivial CLI, web or app based
36	(mobile/non-mobile). In addition, I could see this as an extension to
37	existing programming editors like vscode and zed or even creating our
38	own IDE for vc.
39	
40	See [Initial commit with dual jj-git repos](./notes/chores-01.md#initial-commit-with-dual-jj-git-repos)
41	for how the initial commit was created with the dual jj-git repos. After
42	doing so and I then created this README.md file.
43	
44	## Usage
45	
46	```
47	vc-x1 list [-r REVISION] [-n COMMITS]  # List commits in a jj repo
48	vc-x1 desc [-r REVISION] [-n COMMITS]  # Show full description of a commit
49	vc-x1 chid [-r REVISION] [-n COMMITS]  # Print changeID(s) for a revision
50	vc-x1 show [-r REVISION] [-n COMMITS]  # Show commit details and diff summary
51	vc-x1 validate-desc [OPTS]                 # Validate commit descriptions
52	vc-x1 fix-desc [OPTS]                     # Fix commit descriptions (dry-run default)
53	vc-x1 clone <REPO> [NAME] [OPTS]          # Clone a dual-repo project
54	vc-x1 init <NAME> [OPTS]                  # Create a new dual-repo project
55	vc-x1 symlink [TARGET] [OPTS]             # Create Claude Code project symlink
56	vc-x1 finalize --bookmark <B> [OPTS]       # Squash working copy into target
57	vc-x1 test-fixture [--path PATH]           # Create throwaway jj repo + remote for testing
58	vc-x1 --version                            # Print version
59	vc-x1 --help                           # Print help
60	```
61	
62	**The `..` notation:** dots on the revision show which direction to
63	list commits:
64	
65	- `x..` — x at top, ancestors below (older commits)
66	- `..x` — descendants above (newer commits), x at bottom
67	- `..x..` — both directions, x in the middle
68	
69	COMMITS is the total number of commits to show (x is always included).
70	Without dots, `x` with a count defaults to `x..` (ancestors).
71	For `..x..`, the budget is split: ancestors get the extra on odd counts.
72	
73	```
74	vc-x1 list -r @              # just @ (1 commit)
75	vc-x1 list -r @.. -n 5       # @ + 4 ancestors (5 commits, @ at top)
76	vc-x1 list -r ..@ -n 3       # 2 descendants + @ (3 commits, @ at bottom)
77	vc-x1 list -r ..@.. -n 3     # 1 descendant, @, 1 ancestor (3 commits)
78	vc-x1 list -r ..@.. -n 4     # 1 descendant, @, 2 ancestors (4 commits)
79	```
80	
81	### Shell completion
82	
83	vc-x1 provides tab completion for all subcommands and flags using claps
84	[unstable-dynamic](https://docs.rs/clap_complete/latest/clap_complete/env/struct.CompleteEnv.html)
85	only feature. It is a "simple" implementation and does not handle completing
86	revisions but still useful. To enable it, add one of the following to your shell's startup file:
87	
88	```bash
89	# bash (~/.bashrc)
90	source <(COMPLETE=bash vc-x1)
91	
92	# zsh (~/.zshrc)
93	source <(COMPLETE=zsh vc-x1)
94	
95	# fish (~/.config/fish/config.fish)
96	source (COMPLETE=fish vc-x1 | psub)
97	```
98	
99	Completions are generated dynamically by the binary, so they stay in
100	sync with the installed version automatically.
101	
102	### Positional shorthand
103	
104	REVISION and COMMITS can be given as positional arguments, omitting
105	`-r` and `-n`:
106	
107	```
108	vc-x1 list x                # just x (1 commit)
109	vc-x1 list x 5              # x.. 5 → x + 4 ancestors (5 commits)
110	vc-x1 list x.. 5            # same as above
111	vc-x1 list ..x 3            # 2 descendants + x (3 commits)
112	vc-x1 list ..x.. 3          # 1 descendant, x, 1 ancestor (3 commits)
113	vc-x1 list ..x.. 1          # just x (1 commit)
114	```
115	
116	**Defaults:** with no arguments, REVISION is `@` and COMMITS is 1
117	(just the working copy).
118	
119	Named flags `-r`/`--revision`, `-n`/`--commits`, and `-R`/`--repo`
120	take precedence over positional arguments.
121	
122	### Multi-repo queries
123	
124	The `-R`/`--repo` flag can be repeated or comma-separated to query
125	multiple repos at once. When multiple repos are given, output is
126	labeled with bold `=== path ===` headers by default:
127	
128	```
129	vc-x1 chid -R . -R .claude      # repeated flag
130	vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude         # comma-separated
131	vc-x1 list @.. 3 -R .,.claude   # works with all read-only subcommands
132	```
133	
134	Control the label between repos with `-l`/`--label` and `-L`/`--no-label`:
135	
136	```
137	vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude            # default label: === path ===

139	vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -L         # no label (raw output)
140	```

  "old_string": "### finalize",
  "new_string": "### sync\n\nFetch and sync both repos (`.` and `.claude`) to their remotes in a\nsingle command. Dry-run by default — re-run with `--no-dry-run` to\napply.\n\nPer repo, `sync` classifies the local bookmark against its remote:\n\n| State | Meaning | Action on `--no-dry-run` |\n|------|---------|--------------------------|\n| up-to-date | local == remote | none |\n| behind | local is ancestor of remote | `jj bookmark set <b> -r <b>@<remote>` |\n| ahead | remote is ancestor of local | none (push is a separate step) |\n| diverged | neither is ancestor | `jj rebase -b <local-head> -d <b>@<remote>` |\n| no remote | bookmark has no `@<remote>` counterpart | none — skip |\n\nPre-flight requires a clean working-copy commit (`@` empty — no diff\nfrom `@-`) in every repo. This rules out running `sync` mid-session:\nthe `.claude` repo is non-empty during an active session, and aborting\nsync's revert path would undo that session work.\n\nOn any failure — conflicted rebase, subprocess error, anything — `sync`\nrestores every repo to its starting state via `jj op restore`. Either\nevery repo advances or none do.\n\n```\nvc-x1 sync                # dry-run — report state only\nvc-x1 sync --no-dry-run   # act: fast-forward + rebase as classified\n```\n\n| Flag | Description |\n|------|-------------|\n| `--no-dry-run` | Apply; without it, classify and report only |\n| `--bookmark <NAME>` | Bookmark to sync in each repo [default: main] |\n| `--remote <NAME>` | Remote to sync against [default: origin] |\n\n**Note on the `behind` case.** jj's `git fetch` already fast-forwards a\ntracked local bookmark when it's a strict ancestor of the incoming\nremote, so in the common case `sync` reports `up-to-date` rather than\n`behind`. The `behind` branch covers untracked bookmarks and edge\nconfigs where auto-advance is disabled.\n\n### finalize"
}
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/notes/chores-04.md",
  "old_string": "### Version\n\nSingle-step bump to `0.34.0`. New feature, no breaking changes.",
  "new_string": "### Version\n\nSingle-step bump to `0.34.0`. New feature, no breaking changes.\n\n## Add `sync` subcommand (0.35.0)\n\nSingle-command fetch + sync for both repos (`.` and `.claude`).\nDry-run by default; `--no-dry-run` applies. Rebase-on-divergence is\nautomatic when no conflicts arise; any failure reverts both repos to\ntheir pre-sync state via `jj op restore` so the caller sees an\natomic outcome.\n\n### Flag shape\n\n```\nvc-x1 sync [--no-dry-run] [--bookmark <NAME>] [--remote <NAME>]\n```\n\n- `--no-dry-run` — default is dry-run (classify + report without\n  mutating). User confirmed this default: \"dry-run will be the default\n  until we feel how it goes.\"\n- `--bookmark` — default `main` (the vc-x1 project convention).\n- `--remote` — default `origin`.\n\nRepos are hardcoded: `[\".\", \".claude\"]`. The user's rule is \"always\nboth, at least for now\" — an `-R` override can be added later if the\nneed surfaces.\n\n### Pre-flight: `@` must be empty\n\n`sync` refuses to run if either repo's working-copy commit (`@`) is\nnon-empty. \"Empty\" means no diff from parent (`@ & empty()` revset).\nThat rules out both:\n- The app repo with uncommitted user edits snapshotted into `@`.\n- The `.claude` repo during an active bot session (session jsonl\n  writes make `@` non-empty until finalize squashes them).\n\nThe revert path is `jj op restore <pre-sync-op>`, which rolls back\noperation state across both repos. If `@` had uncommitted work, the\nrevert would undo it — so we refuse up front rather than risk losing\nsession writes.\n\n### States and actions\n\n| State | Detection | `--no-dry-run` action |\n|------|----------|-----------------------|\n| `UpToDate` | `bookmarks(exact:<b>) == {<b>@<remote>}` | none |\n| `Behind` | single local head is strict ancestor of remote | `jj bookmark set <b> -r <b>@<remote>` |\n| `Ahead` | single local head is strict descendant of remote | none (push is separate) |\n| `Diverged` | conflicted bookmark (multiple heads) *or* neither is ancestor | `jj rebase -b <local-only-head> -d <b>@<remote>`, then check `conflicts()` |\n| `NoRemote` | `<b>@<remote>` does not resolve | none — skip |\n\nNote: the `Behind` branch is largely redundant with jj's own\nfetch-auto-fast-forward behavior (tracked bookmarks whose local is a\nstrict ancestor auto-advance on fetch), so in practice the classify\nresult post-fetch is usually `UpToDate`. `Behind` is kept for untracked\nbookmarks and edge configs where auto-advance is disabled.\n\n### `bookmarks(exact:<b>)` for conflicted detection\n\nWhen `jj git fetch` produces a divergence, the local bookmark becomes\nconflicted — it points at *both* its old local tip and the\nfreshly-fetched remote tip. The bare `main` revset then errors with\n`Name \\`main\\` is conflicted`. `bookmarks(exact:main)` returns all\nheads instead, so `classify` can see the multi-head case and classify\nit as `Diverged` directly. The rebase picks the non-remote head from\nthat set as the rebase source.\n\n### Revert on failure\n\nBefore any mutation, `sync` snapshots each repo's current operation id\n(`jj op log -n 1 -T id.short(12)`). On any error, every repo is\nreverted in a best-effort loop (`jj op restore <id>`); revert failures\nare logged as `warn!` but don't override the original error. The caller\nsees the first root-cause error as the process exit value.\n\n### End-to-end verification via `test-fixture`\n\nTested against a dual-repo fixture with a second clone pushing to the\nsame remote:\n\n1. **UpToDate**: sync after fixture creation → both `up-to-date`.\n2. **Pre-flight dirty**: `echo x > f.txt` in work → exit 1 with the\n   \"working-copy commit (@) is not empty\" error.\n3. **Diverged (clean rebase)**: push a commit from the second clone;\n   add an independent commit to work's `main`; run sync. Dry-run\n   classifies `Diverged`, `--no-dry-run` rebases work's commit onto\n   the remote tip; no conflicts; final state is `Ahead` (local\n   contains both commits).\n4. **Ahead**: after step 3, sync reports `ahead, nothing to sync`.\n5. **Diverged with conflict**: both sides modify the same file\n   differently, push remote, run `sync --no-dry-run`. Rebase produces\n   conflicts, `conflicts()` revset detects it, `sync` fails and\n   reverts. Verified no conflicts remain post-revert and\n   `main@origin` is back to its pre-fetch commit.\n\n### Files\n\n- `src/sync.rs` — new module with `SyncArgs`, `State` enum, and\n  `sync()` entry point; classify/act split into small helpers.\n- `src/main.rs` — `mod sync` and `Commands::Sync` dispatch, long_about\n  mirrors the README table.\n- `README.md` — new `### sync` section between `symlink` and\n  `finalize`; usage-table row added.\n- `notes/todo.md` — sync entry moved to Done with ref [46].\n\n### Version\n\nSingle-step bump to `0.35.0`. New feature, no breaking changes. Two\nunit tests on `SyncArgs` parsing; classify/act logic is exercised via\nthe hands-on `test-fixture` flow since the jj subprocess surface is\nhard to mock meaningfully."
}
```

Ready to commit. Here are the two commands for your review — same title, per-repo bodies, ochid trailers cross-linked:

**App repo** (chid `ypnqupwospsn` → `.claude` chid `qpksmolwqtqw`):
```
jj commit -m \
"feat: add sync subcommand (0.35.0)" \
-m "Fetch and sync both repos (\`.\` and \`.claude\`) to their remotes
in a single command. Default is dry-run — \`--no-dry-run\` to act.

Per repo, classify local bookmark vs \`<b>@<remote>\`:
  up-to-date | behind (ff) | ahead | diverged (rebase) | no remote

Pre-flight requires empty \`@\` in each repo, so session writes in
\`.claude\` can't be lost by the revert path. On any failure — conflicted
rebase, subprocess error, anything — both repos are reverted to their
starting state via \`jj op restore\`. Either every repo advances or none.

The diverged case handles jj's conflicted-bookmark shape directly:
after a diverged fetch, \`main\` has two heads; \`bookmarks(exact:main)\`
enumerates them and the non-remote head becomes the rebase source.

ochid: /.claude/qpksmolwqtqw" \
-R .
```

**`.claude` repo** (chid `qpksmolwqtqw` → app chid `ypnqupwospsn`):
```
jj commit -m \
"feat: add sync subcommand (0.35.0)" \
-m "Session: designed sync behavior with the user (hardcoded both repos,
dry-run default, merge-without-conflict is OK, snapshot-and-revert on
any failure). Implemented \`src/sync.rs\`, wired into CLI, verified
end-to-end via \`test-fixture\` across five scenarios: up-to-date,
dirty-@-rejected, diverged with clean rebase, ahead after rebase,
and diverged with conflict (verified revert is clean).

Saved a feedback memory: every fn/method in vc-x1 source needs a
\`///\` doc comment — matches the existing project style.

ochid: /ypnqupwospsn" \
-R .claude
```

Approve to execute both, or tell me what to change.

> DEVELOPER

n

> AGENT

Holding off. What would you like changed — title, body wording, splitting differently, or something else?

> DEVELOPER

I'd be great to have some actual tests, it looked to me you did quite a few tests of different failure modes and we need those so as the code base evolves we have something that might detect errors. Would you agree?

> AGENT

Agreed — the manual runs exercised five scenarios (up-to-date, dirty-@, diverged clean rebase, ahead, diverged with conflict) and none of that is captured in the suite. Those are exactly the regressions worth automating.

Two questions before I write them:

1. **Ship with 0.35.0 or follow-up as 0.35.1?** Nothing's committed yet, so bundling into 0.35.0 keeps "sync came with tests" in one commit — my preference. 0.35.1 is fine too if you'd rather keep the feature commit small.

2. **Test shape.** The cleanest path is a small refactor: extract `sync_repos(repos: &[&Path], args)` taking explicit absolute paths; the CLI `sync()` stays a thin wrapper passing `[".", ".claude"]`. Tests build a real dual-repo fixture (jj subprocess + bare-git remotes, same pattern as `test-fixture`) in a unique tempdir and call `sync_repos` directly. Avoids `chdir` races in parallel tests. The todo already lists this — *"Add integration tests in tests/ for subcommands using temp jj repos"*.

Note: these tests require `jj` in `PATH` at test time (my sync.rs shells out), which is a new property for this repo. Acceptable?

> DEVELOPER

1. single commit is fine
2. refactoring to assist with testing is what I'd expect and all the more reason to do it now, IIUC

171	    let code_chid = jj_chid("@-", &work_dir)?;
172	    let session_chid = jj_chid("@-", &session_dir)?;
173	    let code_desc = format!("initial commit\n\nochid: /.claude/{session_chid}");
174	    let session_desc = format!("initial commit\n\nochid: /{code_chid}");
175	    run("jj", &["describe", "@-", "-m", &code_desc], &work_dir)?;
176	    run("jj", &["describe", "@-", "-m", &session_desc], &session_dir)?;
177	
178	    info!("Step 6: Setting bookmarks and wiring remotes...");
179	    run("jj", &["bookmark", "set", "main", "-r", "@-"], &work_dir)?;
180	    run("jj", &["bookmark", "set", "main", "-r", "@-"], &session_dir)?;
181	    let code_url = remote_code.canonicalize()?.to_string_lossy().into_owned();
182	    let claude_url = remote_claude.canonicalize()?.to_string_lossy().into_owned();
183	    run("git", &["remote", "add", "origin", &code_url], &work_dir)?;
184	    run(
185	        "git",
186	        &["remote", "add", "origin", &claude_url],
187	        &session_dir,
188	    )?;
189	
190	    info!("Step 7: Pushing main to both remotes...");
191	    run("jj", &["git", "push", "--bookmark", "main"], &work_dir)?;
192	    run("jj", &["git", "push", "--bookmark", "main"], &session_dir)?;
193	
194	    if args.with_pending {
195	        info!("Step 8: Writing uncommitted changes to @ (both repos)...");
196	        write_file(&work_dir.join("TODO.md"), "# TODO\n- first feature\n")?;
197	        write_file(
198	            &session_dir.join("session-notes.md"),
199	            "# Session notes\n- simulated pending work\n",
200	        )?;
201	    }
202	
203	    info!("");
204	    info!("Fixture ready (local bare-git remotes, see README.md § test-fixture):");
205	    info!("  Code repo:     {}", work_dir.display());
206	    info!("  Session repo:  {}", session_dir.display());
207	    info!("  Code remote:   {}", remote_code.display());
208	    info!("  Claude remote: {}", remote_claude.display());
209	    info!("");
210	    info!("Next steps — see README.md § Testing push + finalize for the full flow.");
211	    info!("Quick reference with this fixture's paths:");
212	    info!("  jj git push -R {}", work_dir.display());
213	    info!(
214	        "  vc-x1 finalize --repo {} --squash --push main --detach",
215	        session_dir.display()
216	    );
217	    info!("  vc-x1 test-fixture-rm {}", base.display());
218	
219	    debug!("test_fixture: exit");
220	    Ok(())
221	}

use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
use std::time::{SystemTime, UNIX_EPOCH};

use clap::Args;
use log::{debug, info};

use crate::common::{mkdir_p, run, write_file};
use crate::init::{
    copy_template_recursive, parse_use_template, rewrite_readme_first_line, validate_templates,
};

/// Create a throwaway dual-repo jj fixture for testing.
///
/// Mirrors the real dual-repo layout created by `vc-x1 init` minus the
/// GitHub side and the `~/.claude/projects/` symlink: app repo at
/// `<base>/work/`, bot session repo at `<base>/work/.claude/`, and a local
/// bare-git remote per repo under `<base>/remote-*.git/`. Both repos carry
/// `.vc-config.toml`, `.gitignore`, an initial described commit with
/// matching `ochid:` trailers, and a tracked `main` bookmark.
///
/// The remotes are local bare-git directories inside the fixture — no
/// network, no GitHub. See README.md § test-fixture for details.
#[derive(Args, Debug)]
pub struct TestFixtureArgs {
    /// Fixture root directory [default: $TMPDIR/vc-x1-test-<timestamp>]
    #[arg(long, value_name = "PATH")]
    pub path: Option<PathBuf>,

    /// Leave uncommitted changes in @ so finalize --squash has real work
    #[arg(long)]
    pub with_pending: bool,

    /// Seed both repos from template directories.
    ///
    /// Same format and semantics as `vc-x1 init --use-template`:
    /// `CODE[,BOT]`. If `BOT` is omitted, defaults to the sibling directory
    /// `<CODE>.claude`. Non-hidden contents are copied recursively; hidden
    /// entries are skipped. `README.md` at the root of each target (if
    /// present) gets its first line rewritten to `# <repo-name>`, where
    /// the code repo is `work` and the bot repo is `work.claude`.
    #[arg(long, value_name = "CODE[,BOT]")]
    pub use_template: Option<String>,
}

/// Remove a test fixture. Refuses anything whose directory name doesn't
/// start with `vc-x1-test-`, so it cannot chew up arbitrary paths.
#[derive(Args, Debug)]
pub struct TestFixtureRmArgs {
    /// Fixture root directory to remove
    #[arg(value_name = "PATH")]
    pub path: PathBuf,
}

const VC_CONFIG_CODE: &str = r#"# vc-config: Vibe Coding workspace configuration
[workspace]
path = "/"
other-repo = ".claude"
"#;

const VC_CONFIG_SESSION: &str = r#"# vc-config: Vibe Coding workspace configuration
[workspace]
path = "/.claude"
other-repo = ".."
"#;

const GITIGNORE_CODE: &str = "/target
/.claude
/.git
/.jj
";

const GITIGNORE_SESSION: &str = ".git
.jj
";

55	/// Entry point for the `sync` subcommand.
56	///
57	/// Orchestrates the full flow: pre-flight clean-check on every repo,
58	/// snapshot each repo's current op id, then hand off to `run_plan` for
59	/// fetch + classify + (optional) act. On any error, revert every repo
60	/// to its snapshot op via `jj op restore` so the caller sees an atomic
61	/// "either it all went through or nothing changed" outcome.
62	pub fn sync(args: &SyncArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
63	    debug!(
64	        "sync: enter (no_dry_run={}, bookmark={}, remote={})",
65	        args.no_dry_run, args.bookmark, args.remote
66	    );
67	
68	    let repos: Vec<PathBuf> = REPOS.iter().map(PathBuf::from).collect();
69	
70	    // Pre-flight: every repo must exist, be a jj repo, and have a clean @.
71	    for repo in &repos {
72	        check_clean(repo)?;
73	    }
74	
75	    // Snapshot starting op id per repo — used to revert on any failure.
76	    let mut snapshots: Vec<(PathBuf, String)> = Vec::new();
77	    for repo in &repos {
78	        let op_id = current_op_id(repo)?;
79	        debug!("{}: op snapshot = {op_id}", repo.display());
80	        snapshots.push((repo.clone(), op_id));
81	    }
82	
83	    let result = run_plan(&snapshots, args);
84	
85	    if let Err(e) = &result {
86	        warn!("sync failed: {e}");
87	        warn!("reverting all repos to starting state...");
88	        for (repo, op_id) in &snapshots {
89	            match op_restore(repo, op_id) {
90	                Ok(()) => info!("  {}: reverted to op {op_id}", repo.display()),
91	                Err(re) => warn!("  {}: revert failed: {re}", repo.display()),
92	            }
93	        }
94	    }
95	
96	    debug!("sync: exit");
97	    result
98	}

100	/// Fetch and classify each repo, then act (or not, in dry-run).
101	///
102	/// Returns `Err` on the first failure so the caller can trigger the
103	/// cross-repo revert; partial success across repos is *not* a valid
104	/// end state for this command.
105	fn run_plan(
106	    snapshots: &[(PathBuf, String)],
107	    args: &SyncArgs,
108	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
109	    let mut ctxs: Vec<RepoCtx> = Vec::new();
110	    for (repo, op_id) in snapshots {
111	        info!("{}: fetch {}", repo.display(), args.remote);
112	        run(
113	            "jj",
114	            &[
115	                "git",
116	                "fetch",
117	                "--remote",
118	                &args.remote,
119	                "-R",
120	                &repo_str(repo),
121	            ],
122	            Path::new("."),
123	        )?;
124	        let state = classify(repo, &args.bookmark, &args.remote)?;
125	        log_state(repo, &state);
126	        ctxs.push(RepoCtx {
127	            path: repo.clone(),
128	            op_id: op_id.clone(),
129	            state,
130	        });
131	    }
132	
133	    for ctx in &ctxs {
134	        act_on_state(ctx, args)?;
135	    }
136	
137	    if !args.no_dry_run {
138	        info!("dry-run — re-run with --no-dry-run to apply");
139	    }
140	    Ok(())
141	}
