On any error, revert every repo\n/// to its snapshot op via `jj op restore` so the caller sees an atomic\n/// \"either it all went through or nothing changed\" outcome.\npub fn sync(args: &SyncArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    debug!(\n        \"sync: enter (no_dry_run={}, bookmark={}, remote={})\",\n        args.no_dry_run, args.bookmark, args.remote\n    );\n\n    let repos: Vec<PathBuf> = REPOS.iter().map(PathBuf::from).collect();\n\n    // Pre-flight: every repo must exist, be a jj repo, and have a clean @.\n    for repo in &repos {\n        check_clean(repo)?;\n    }\n\n    // Snapshot starting op id per repo — used to revert on any failure.\n    let mut snapshots: Vec<(PathBuf, String)> = Vec::new();\n    for repo in &repos {\n        let op_id = current_op_id(repo)?;\n        debug!(\"{}: op snapshot = {op_id}\", repo.display());\n        snapshots.push((repo.clone(), op_id));\n    }\n\n    let result = run_plan(&snapshots, args);\n\n    if let Err(e) = &result {\n        warn!(\"sync failed: {e}\");\n        warn!(\"reverting all repos to starting state...\");\n        for (repo, op_id) in &snapshots {\n            match op_restore(repo, op_id) {\n                Ok(()) => info!(\"  {}: reverted to op {op_id}\", repo.display()),\n                Err(re) => warn!(\"  {}: revert failed: {re}\", repo.display()),\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    debug!(\"sync: exit\");\n    result\n}",
  "new_string": "/// CLI entry point for the `sync` subcommand.\n///\n/// Thin wrapper over `sync_repos` that supplies the project's\n/// hardcoded repo pair `[\".\" \".claude\"]`. Tests call `sync_repos`\n/// directly with absolute fixture paths.\npub fn sync(args: &SyncArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let repos: Vec<PathBuf> = REPOS.iter().map(PathBuf::from).collect();\n    sync_repos(&repos, args)\n}\n\n/// Sync the given repos against their remotes.\n///\n/// Orchestrates the full flow: pre-flight clean-check on every repo,\n/// snapshot each repo's current op id, then hand off to `run_plan` for\n/// fetch + classify + (optional) act. On any error, revert every repo\n/// to its snapshot op via `jj op restore` so the caller sees an atomic\n/// \"either it all went through or nothing changed\" outcome.\n///\n/// Paths may be relative (resolved against the process cwd) or\n/// absolute. Tests use absolute tempdir paths to avoid cwd dependence\n/// under parallel `cargo test`.\npub fn sync_repos(\n    repos: &[PathBuf],\n    args: &SyncArgs,\n) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    debug!(\n        \"sync: enter (no_dry_run={}, bookmark={}, remote={})\",\n        args.no_dry_run, args.bookmark, args.remote\n    );\n\n    for repo in repos {\n        check_clean(repo)?;\n    }\n\n    let mut snapshots: Vec<(PathBuf, String)> = Vec::new();\n    for repo in repos {\n        let op_id = current_op_id(repo)?;\n        debug!(\"{}: op snapshot = {op_id}\", repo.display());\n        snapshots.push((repo.clone(), op_id));\n    }\n\n    let result = run_plan(&snapshots, args);\n\n    if let Err(e) = &result {\n        warn!(\"sync failed: {e}\");\n        warn!(\"reverting all repos to starting state...\");\n        for (repo, op_id) in &snapshots {\n            match op_restore(repo, op_id) {\n                Ok(()) => info!(\"  {}: reverted to op {op_id}\", repo.display()),\n                Err(re) => warn!(\"  {}: revert failed: {re}\", repo.display()),\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    debug!(\"sync: exit\");\n    result\n}"
}

End-to-end tests for `sync_repos` against real dual-repo jj\n    //! fixtures. Each test builds an isolated fixture (bare-git remotes\n    //! + colocated jj repos) under a unique tempdir via\n    //! `crate::test_fixture::test_fixture`, then drives the scenario\n    //! with plain `jj` subprocess calls. Requires `jj` in `PATH`.\n    //!\n    //! Fixtures clean themselves up via `Fixture`'s `Drop` impl so a\n    //! panicking test still removes its tempdir.\n\n    use super::*;\n    use crate::test_fixture::{TestFixtureArgs, test_fixture};\n    use std::fs;\n    use std::process::Command;\n    use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicU64, Ordering};\n    use std::time::{SystemTime, UNIX_EPOCH};\n\n    static COUNTER: AtomicU64 = AtomicU64::new(0);\n\n    /// Build a unique tempdir path for a test fixture.\n    ///\n    /// Combines a nanosecond timestamp with a per-process atomic\n    /// counter so parallel tests and same-nanosecond collisions both\n    /// yield distinct paths.\n    fn unique_base(tag: &str) -> PathBuf {\n        let ts = SystemTime::now()\n            .duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH)\n            .map(|d| d.as_nanos())\n            .unwrap_or(0);\n        let n = COUNTER.fetch_add(1, Ordering::SeqCst);\n        std::env::temp_dir().join(format!(\"vc-x1-sync-{tag}-{ts}-{n}\"))\n    }\n\n    /// Owned dual-repo fixture with RAII cleanup.\n    struct Fixture {\n        base: PathBuf,\n        work: PathBuf,\n        claude: PathBuf,\n    }\n\n    impl Fixture {\n        /// Build a fresh fixture in a unique tempdir.\n        fn new(tag: &str) -> Self {\n            let base = unique_base(tag);\n            let args = TestFixtureArgs {\n                path: Some(base.clone()),\n                with_pending: false,\n                use_template: None,\n            };\n            test_fixture(&args).expect(\"build test fixture\");\n            let work = base.join(\"work\");\n            let claude = work.join(\".claude\");\n            Fixture { base, work, claude }\n        }\n\n        /// Repos to pass to `sync_repos`.\n        fn repos(&self) -> Vec<PathBuf> {\n            vec![self.work.clone(), self.claude.clone()]\n        }\n    }\n\n    impl Drop for Fixture {\n        /// Remove the fixture tree on drop. Best-effort; a failure here\n        /// doesn't fail the test.\n        fn drop(&mut self) {\n            let _ = fs::remove_dir_all(&self.base);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Run `jj <args> -R <repo>` and assert success; returns trimmed stdout.\n    fn jj(repo: &Path, args: &[&str]) -> String {\n        let out = Command::new(\"jj\")\n            .args(args)\n            .arg(\"-R\")\n            .arg(repo)\n            .output()\n            .expect(\"spawn jj\");\n        assert!(\n            out.status.success(),\n            \"jj {args:?} failed in {}: {}\",\n            repo.display(),\n            String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stderr)\n        );\n        String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stdout).trim().to_string()\n    }\n\n    /// Resolve `rev` to its short commit id in `repo`.\n    fn cid(repo: &Path, rev: &str) -> String {\n        jj(\n            repo,\n            &[\n                \"log\",\n                \"-r\",\n                rev,\n                \"--no-graph\",\n                \"-T\",\n                \"commit_id.short(12)\",\n            ],\n        )\n    }\n\n    /// Default sync args: dry-run, bookmark `main`, remote `origin`.\n    fn default_args() -> SyncArgs {\n        SyncArgs {\n            no_dry_run: false,\n            bookmark: \"main\".to_string(),\n            remote: \"origin\".to_string(),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Sync args with `--no-dry-run` set.\n    fn apply_args() -> SyncArgs {\n        SyncArgs {\n            no_dry_run: true,\n            bookmark: \"main\".to_string(),\n            remote: \"origin\".to_string(),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Add a local-only commit on `main` in `repo` (not pushed), then\n    /// restore `@` to an empty child so pre-flight still passes.\n    ///\n    /// Sequence: write file → describe `@` → advance `main` to `@` →\n    /// create a fresh empty `@` above it.\n    fn add_local_commit(repo: &Path, file: &str, content: &str, msg: &str) {\n        fs::write(repo.join(file), content).expect(\"write local file\");\n        jj(repo, &[\"describe\", \"@\", \"-m\", msg]);\n        jj(repo, &[\"bookmark\", \"set\", \"main\", \"-r\", \"@\"]);\n        jj(repo, &[\"new\"]);\n    }\n\n    /// Clone `remote_url` into `<base>/<work_name>`, add a commit, push it.\n    ///\n    /// Used to make the remote advance beyond the fixture's `main`\n    /// from a separate working copy. Returns the pushed commit's id.\n    fn push_from_clone(\n        base: &Path,\n        remote_url: &Path,\n        work_name: &str,\n        file: &str,\n        content: &str,\n        msg: &str,\n    ) -> String {\n        let workdir = base.join(work_name);\n        let out = Command::new(\"jj\")\n            .args([\"git\", \"clone\", \"--colocate\"])\n            .arg(remote_url)\n            .arg(&workdir)\n            .output()\n            .expect(\"spawn jj clone\");\n        assert!(\n            out.status.success(),\n            \"jj git clone failed: {}\",\n            String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stderr)\n        );\n        fs::write(workdir.join(file), content).expect(\"write remote file\");\n        jj(&workdir, &[\"describe\", \"@\", \"-m\", msg]);\n        jj(&workdir, &[\"bookmark\", \"set\", \"main\", \"-r\", \"@\"]);\n        jj(&workdir, &[\"git\", \"push\", \"--bookmark\", \"main\"]);\n        cid(&workdir, \"main\")\n    }\n\n    /// Scenario 1: fresh fixture, nothing to do — `sync` leaves both\n    /// repos untouched.\n    #[test]\n    fn sync_up_to_date() {\n        let fx = Fixture::new(\"up-to-date\");\n        let work_main = cid(&fx.work, \"main\");\n        let claude_main = cid(&fx.claude, \"main\");\n        sync_repos(&fx.repos(), &apply_args()).expect(\"sync should succeed\");\n        assert_eq!(cid(&fx.work, \"main\"), work_main);\n        assert_eq!(cid(&fx.claude, \"main\"), claude_main);\n    }\n\n    /// Scenario 2: a non-empty `@` in either repo causes pre-flight\n    /// to refuse the whole operation.\n    #[test]\n    fn sync_preflight_rejects_dirty_at() {\n        let fx = Fixture::new(\"dirty-at\");\n        fs::write(fx.work.join(\"dirty.txt\"), \"pending work\").expect(\"write dirty file\");\n        let err = sync_repos(&fx.repos(), &default_args())\n            .unwrap_err()\n            .to_string();\n        assert!(\n            err.contains(\"working-copy commit (@) is not empty\"),\n            \"unexpected error: {err}\"\n        );\n    }\n\n    /// Scenario 3: local has commits not yet pushed; sync classifies\n    /// `ahead` and leaves the local bookmark alone even under\n    /// `--no-dry-run`.\n    #[test]\n    fn sync_ahead_is_noop() {\n        let fx = Fixture::new(\"ahead\");\n        add_local_commit(&fx.work, \"local.txt\", \"local\\n\", \"feat: local only\");\n        let ahead_head = cid(&fx.work, \"main\");\n        sync_repos(&fx.repos(), &apply_args()).expect(\"sync should succeed\");\n        assert_eq!(cid(&fx.work, \"main\"), ahead_head);\n    }\n\n    /// Scenario 4: clean divergence — both sides advance main on\n    /// different files; sync rebases local onto remote and the result\n    /// is conflict-free.\n    #[test]\n    fn sync_diverged_rebases() {\n        let fx = Fixture::new(\"diverged\");\n        let remote_code = fx.base.join(\"remote-code.git\");\n        let remote_head = push_from_clone(\n            &fx.base,\n            &remote_code,\n            \"work2\",\n            \"remote.txt\",\n            \"remote\\n\",\n            \"feat: remote only\",\n        );\n        add_local_commit(&fx.work, \"local.txt\", \"local\\n\", \"feat: local only\");\n\n        sync_repos(&fx.repos(), &apply_args()).expect(\"sync should succeed\");\n\n        // Remote tracking bookmark now points at the pushed remote commit.\n        assert_eq!(\n            cid(&fx.work, \"main@origin\"),\n            remote_head,\n            \"main@origin should match pushed remote commit\"\n        );\n        // Local main is ahead of remote — rebased local commit sits on top.\n        let main_after = cid(&fx.work, \"main\");\n        assert_ne!(\n            main_after, remote_head,\n            \"local main should be ahead of remote after rebase\"\n        );\n        // Remote is an ancestor of local post-rebase.\n        let anc = jj(\n            &fx.work,\n            &[\n                \"log\",\n                \"-r\",\n                &format!(\"{remote_head}::{main_after}\"),\n                \"--no-graph\",\n                \"-T\",\n                r#\"commit_id.short(12) ++ \"\\n\"\"#,\n            ],\n        );\n        assert!(\n            !anc.trim().is_empty(),\n            \"remote should be ancestor of local after rebase\"\n        );\n        // No conflicts.\n        let conflicts = jj(\n            &fx.work,\n            &[\n                \"log\",\n                \"-r\",\n                \"conflicts()\",\n                \"--no-graph\",\n                \"-T\",\n                r#\"commit_id ++ \"\\n\"\"#,\n            ],\n        );\n        assert!(conflicts.trim().is_empty(), \"no conflicts expected\");\n    }\n\n    /// Scenario 5: conflicting divergence — both sides modify the\n    /// same path differently. Rebase produces conflicts; sync fails\n    /// and reverts both repos to their pre-sync state.\n    #[test]\n    fn sync_diverged_conflict_reverts() {\n        let fx = Fixture::new(\"conflict\");\n        let remote_code = fx.base.join(\"remote-code.git\");\n        push_from_clone(\n            &fx.base,\n            &remote_code,\n            \"work2\",\n            \"shared.txt\",\n            \"remote-version\\n\",\n            \"feat: remote shared\",\n        );\n        add_local_commit(\n            &fx.work,\n            \"shared.txt\",\n            \"local-version\\n\",\n            \"feat: local shared (conflicting)\",\n        );\n\n        let pre_main = cid(&fx.work, \"main\");\n        let pre_remote = cid(&fx.work, \"main@origin\");\n\n        let err = sync_repos(&fx.repos(), &apply_args())\n            .unwrap_err()\n            .to_string();\n        assert!(\n            err.contains(\"rebase produced conflicts\"),\n            \"unexpected error: {err}\"\n        );\n\n        // After revert: main and main@origin are back where they started.\n        assert_eq!(cid(&fx.work, \"main\"), pre_main, \"main should be reverted\");\n        assert_eq!(\n            cid(&fx.work, \"main@origin\"),\n            pre_remote,\n            \"main@origin should be reverted (pre-fetch state)\"\n        );\n        // No conflicts remain.\n        let conflicts = jj(\n            &fx.work,\n            &[\n                \"log\",\n                \"-r\",\n                \"conflicts()\",\n                \"--no-graph\",\n                \"-T\",\n                r#\"commit_id ++ \"\\n\"\"#,\n            ],\n        );\n        assert!(\n            conflicts.trim().is_empty(),\n            \"no conflicts should remain after revert\"\n        );\n    }\n}"
}
```

{

  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/notes/chores-04.md",
  "old_string": "### End-to-end verification via `test-fixture`\n\nTested against a dual-repo fixture with a second clone pushing to the\nsame remote:\n\n1. **UpToDate**: sync after fixture creation → both `up-to-date`.\n2. **Pre-flight dirty**: `echo x > f.txt` in work → exit 1 with the\n   \"working-copy commit (@) is not empty\" error.\n3. **Diverged (clean rebase)**: push a commit from the second clone;\n   add an independent commit to work's `main`; run sync. Dry-run\n   classifies `Diverged`, `--no-dry-run` rebases work's commit onto\n   the remote tip; no conflicts; final state is `Ahead` (local\n   contains both commits).\n4. **Ahead**: after step 3, sync reports `ahead, nothing to sync`.\n5. **Diverged with conflict**: both sides modify the same file\n   differently, push remote, run `sync --no-dry-run`. Rebase produces\n   conflicts, `conflicts()` revset detects it, `sync` fails and\n   reverts. Verified no conflicts remain post-revert and\n   `main@origin` is back to its pre-fetch commit.",
  "new_string": "### Integration tests (`src/sync.rs::integration_tests`)\n\nAll five scenarios are automated as `#[test]` functions that build\nisolated dual-repo fixtures via `crate::test_fixture::test_fixture`\nunder unique tempdirs, then drive the scenario with `jj` subprocess\ncalls and assert on the resulting state. Fixtures clean themselves up\nvia a `Drop` impl so panicking tests still remove their tempdirs.\n\nTests require `jj` in `PATH` at test time — new property for this\nrepo, but `sync` itself shells out to `jj` so the tests can't avoid\nthe dependency.\n\n- **`sync_up_to_date`** — fresh fixture, `--no-dry-run`; assert both\n  repos' `main` commit ids unchanged.\n- **`sync_preflight_rejects_dirty_at`** — write a file into `work/`\n  so `@` becomes non-empty on next snapshot; assert error string\n  contains \"working-copy commit (@) is not empty\".\n- **`sync_ahead_is_noop`** — add a local commit on `main`,\n  `--no-dry-run`; assert `main` commit id unchanged after sync.\n- **`sync_diverged_rebases`** — a second clone pushes a commit to\n  `remote-code.git`; local adds a different commit on `main`;\n  `--no-dry-run` triggers the rebase path. Asserts:\n  - `main@origin` advanced to the pushed remote commit.\n  - Local `main` is a different id than before (rebased).\n  - Remote head is an ancestor of local `main`.\n  - `conflicts()` revset is empty.\n- **`sync_diverged_conflict_reverts`** — both sides write different\n  contents to the same path; rebase produces conflicts; sync errors\n  with \"rebase produced conflicts\"; assert both `main` and\n  `main@origin` are back at their pre-sync ids and `conflicts()`\n  revset is empty (so the revert was clean across fetch + rebase).\n\nTotal run time ~1s for the five-test module on a local workstation.\n\n### Refactor for testability: `sync_repos`\n\nExtracted `pub fn sync_repos(repos: &[PathBuf], args)` from the\noriginal `sync()`. CLI `sync()` is now a thin wrapper that builds\n`[\".\", \".claude\"]` and delegates; tests pass absolute tempdir paths\nto `sync_repos` directly. Avoids `chdir` races under parallel\n`cargo test` and keeps the CLI API unchanged."
}

All 165 tests pass. Revised commit commands with the tests and refactor noted:

**App repo** (chid `ypnqupwospsn` → `.claude` chid `qpksmolwqtqw`):
```
jj commit -m \
"feat: add sync subcommand (0.35.0)" \
-m "Fetch and sync both repos (\`.\` and \`.claude\`) to their remotes
in a single command. Default is dry-run — \`--no-dry-run\` to act.

Per repo, classify local bookmark vs \`<b>@<remote>\`:
  up-to-date | behind (ff) | ahead | diverged (rebase) | no remote

Pre-flight requires empty \`@\` in each repo, so session writes in
\`.claude\` can't be lost by the revert path. On any failure — conflicted
rebase, subprocess error, anything — both repos are reverted to their
starting state via \`jj op restore\`. Either every repo advances or none.

The diverged case handles jj's conflicted-bookmark shape directly:
after a diverged fetch, \`main\` has two heads; \`bookmarks(exact:main)\`
enumerates them and the non-remote head becomes the rebase source.

Testability: \`sync()\` is a thin CLI wrapper over \`sync_repos(repos,
args)\`, so integration tests pass absolute tempdir paths and avoid
\`chdir\` races under parallel \`cargo test\`. Five new integration tests
cover up-to-date, dirty-@ pre-flight, ahead, diverged clean rebase,
and diverged-with-conflict revert, driven against real dual-repo
fixtures built via \`test_fixture\`. Requires \`jj\` in \`PATH\` at test
time.

ochid: /.claude/qpksmolwqtqw" \
-R .
```

**`.claude` repo** (chid `qpksmolwqtqw` → app chid `ypnqupwospsn`):

dry-run default, merge-without-conflict is OK, snapshot-and-revert on
any failure). Implemented \`src/sync.rs\`, refactored into
\`sync_repos(repos, args)\` for testability, added five integration
tests covering the classify/revert paths end-to-end against real
dual-repo fixtures. Verified hands-on via \`test-fixture\` before and
after automating.

Saved a feedback memory: every fn/method in vc-x1 source needs a
\`///\` doc comment — matches the existing project style.

ochid: /ypnqupwospsn" \
-R .claude
```

> DEVELOPER

I think there is going to be a problem, at the end of a session and we've commited everything we've got two clean directories, but when I do an /exit you there will be modifications in .claude, 5 or so lines added to one or more .claude/xxx.jsonl files. I think that means `vc-x1 sync ..` will always fail the most typical case.

> AGENT

You're right — that's a real gap. After `/exit` writes trailing jsonl lines, `.claude`'s `@` is non-empty, and the "typical" next sync would fail pre-flight.

Three possible resolutions:

1. **Relax pre-flight.** jj's `op restore` on rebase-rollback *should* preserve the working-copy content — the files stay; only operation history is rewound, and the re-snapshot into the new `@` carries the content forward. If that's true for all our failure paths, the empty-@ check was overcautious. I haven't verified empirically; it's testable with a fixture.

2. **Sync auto-squashes `@` into `@-` for `.claude`** (finalize-style). Matches the existing pattern but needs `--ignore-immutable` when `@-` is already pushed, and it changes `.claude` history every sync.

3. **`--allow-dirty` flag**, default strict. Explicit opt-in, less clever.

Leaning toward (1) — the cleanest if it actually works. Since the commits aren't pushed yet, I can verify with a fixture test, then amend. Shall I go test it?

> DEVELOPER

tell me what you learned

> AGENT

What I confirmed and what's still open:

**Confirmed**
1. **`jj git fetch` auto-advances tracked `main` to the remote tip when local is a strict ancestor** — the "Behind" branch in my classify is redundant in the tracked-bookmark common case.

2. **@ does NOT follow the auto-advance.** Before fetch: `@` child of old main. After fetch: main moves to new remote commit, but `@`'s parent is still the old main. `@` is now dangling off a stale point in history, not on main's line.

3. **@'s working-copy content (the trailing `.jsonl`) survives both fetch and a manual `jj rebase -s @ -d main`.** The rebase gave `@` a new commit id but the file stayed on disk with correct content.

**Implication for sync**
The simple "relax pre-flight" isn't enough. After fetch (or after a diverged rebase of main), **`@` itself must be rebased onto the new main** or the user's trailing writes end up orphaned. So sync needs an extra step: `jj rebase -s 'roots((old_main::@) ~ (new_main::))' -d new_main` or similar — move anything that was above old_main onto new_main, including `@`.

**Not yet verified**
The revert path: does `jj op restore` cleanly roll back across a failed rebase when `@` was non-empty, leaving `@`'s content intact with no conflict markers? That's the next test, and the one that matters most — if it works, option (1) is viable; if not, we need a different approach.

Want me to run that test?

> AGENT

Both paths verified. Summary:

**Success path (empirical):**
- `jj git fetch` auto-ff's main *but leaves @ dangling off the old main commit.*
- `jj rebase -s @ -d main` moves @ forward; @'s content (trailing.jsonl) is preserved, @ gets a new commit id.

**Failure path (empirical — just ran):**
- Setup: local main has a local "shared.txt" commit; @ above main has trailing.jsonl; remote pushes a conflicting "shared.txt" commit.
- Fetch → main becomes conflicted (both heads). Rebase → produces 2 conflicted commits (the rebased local commit + the rebased @).
- `jj op restore <pre-sync-op>` → all four checks pass:
  - `main` back at pre-sync id
  - `main@origin` back at pre-fetch id
  - `@` back at **the exact same commit id** as before sync (not a new revert commit)
  - `conflicts()` revset empty
  - `trailing.jsonl` still on disk with correct content
  - `shared.txt` has local content (no conflict markers)
- jj prints: `Existing conflicts were resolved or abandoned from 2 commits.`

**Conclusion**
Option 1 is viable. Two changes needed:
1. Drop the empty-@ pre-flight.
2. Add an explicit "rebase `@` onto main" step after fetch — otherwise @ dangles off old main even in the plain auto-ff case. `jj rebase -s @ -d main` (guarded by "only if @ isn't already a descendant of main"). Op restore cleanly unwinds this too if it conflicts.

Want me to amend the not-yet-pushed 0.35.0 commits with this change + tests for the trailing-@ flow?

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/sync.rs",
  "old_string": "    for ctx in &ctxs {\n        act_on_state(ctx, args)?;\n    }\n\n    if !args.no_dry_run {\n        info!(\"dry-run — re-run with --no-dry-run to apply\");\n    }\n    Ok(())\n}",
  "new_string": "    for ctx in &ctxs {\n        act_on_state(ctx, args)?;\n        ensure_at_on_main(&ctx.path, &args.bookmark, args.no_dry_run)?;\n    }\n\n    if !args.no_dry_run {\n        info!(\"dry-run — re-run with --no-dry-run to apply\");\n    }\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n/// Ensure `@` is a descendant of `bookmark`, rebasing if not.\n///\n/// `jj git fetch` fast-forwards a tracked local bookmark to the remote\n/// tip when local is a strict ancestor, but leaves `@` behind — still\n/// parented on the *pre-fetch* bookmark commit. The `.claude` repo is\n/// the motivating case: trailing session writes (e.g. `/exit`'s jsonl\n/// tail) sit in `@`, and without this step they'd end up orphaned on a\n/// stale branch when the remote advanced.\n///\n/// Skipped when `main::@` is already non-empty (i.e., `@` is already\n/// reachable from `bookmark`). On `--no-dry-run`, runs\n/// `jj rebase -b @ -d <bookmark>` which carries all commits between\n/// the old parent and `@` forward, then checks `conflicts()` — any\n/// conflict in this step trips the outer revert the same way a\n/// conflicted `act_on_state` rebase would.\nfn ensure_at_on_main(\n    repo: &Path,\n    bookmark: &str,\n    apply: bool,\n) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    if revset_nonempty(repo, &format!(\"{bookmark}::@\"))? {\n        return Ok(());\n    }\n    info!(\"{}: rebasing @ onto '{bookmark}'\", repo.display());\n    if apply {\n        run(\n            \"jj\",\n            &[\n                \"rebase\",\n                \"-b\",\n                \"@\",\n                \"-d\",\n                bookmark,\n                \"-R\",\n                &repo_str(repo),\n            ],\n            Path::new(\".\"),\n        )?;\n        if has_conflicts(repo)? {\n            return Err(format!(\n                \"{}: rebase of @ onto '{bookmark}' produced conflicts\",\n                repo.display()\n            )\n            .into());\n        }\n    }\n    Ok(())\n}"
}

String: /// Fail if `repo`'s working-copy commit (`@`) is not empty.
///
/// "Empty" means no diff from parent (`@ & empty()` revset). The
/// `.claude` repo is non-empty during an active bot session, and the
/// app repo is non-empty when the user has uncommitted edits; in
/// either case, sync's revert path (via `jj op restore`) would roll
/// state back across that work, so we refuse up front.
fn check_clean(repo: &Path) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    if !revset_nonempty(repo, "@ & empty()")? {
        return Err(format!(
            "{}: working-copy commit (@) is not empty — commit or abandon first",
            repo.display()
        )
        .into());
    }
    Ok(())
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/sync.rs",
  "old_string": "    /// Scenario 2: a non-empty `@` in either repo causes pre-flight\n    /// to refuse the whole operation.\n    #[test]\n    fn sync_preflight_rejects_dirty_at() {\n        let fx = Fixture::new(\"dirty-at\");\n        fs::write(fx.work.join(\"dirty.txt\"), \"pending work\").expect(\"write dirty file\");\n        let err = sync_repos(&fx.repos(), &default_args())\n            .unwrap_err()\n            .to_string();\n        assert!(\n            err.contains(\"working-copy commit (@) is not empty\"),\n            \"unexpected error: {err}\"\n        );\n    }",
  "new_string": "    /// Scenario 2a: a non-empty `@` on top of main (simulates `/exit`\n    /// trailing session writes in `.claude`) is fine when there's\n    /// nothing new on the remote — sync is still a no-op.\n    #[test]\n    fn sync_tolerates_trailing_at_up_to_date() {\n        let fx = Fixture::new(\"trailing-uptodate\");\n        let pre_at = cid(&fx.claude, \"@\");\n        fs::write(fx.claude.join(\"trailing.jsonl\"), \"{\\\"line\\\":1}\\n\")\n            .expect(\"write trailing file\");\n        // No fetch/advance; sync should leave @ where it is (already on main's line).\n        sync_repos(&fx.repos(), &apply_args()).expect(\"sync should succeed\");\n        assert_eq!(\n            cid(&fx.claude, \"@\"),\n            pre_at,\n            \"@ should be unchanged when there's nothing to rebase\"\n        );\n        assert_eq!(\n            fs::read_to_string(fx.claude.join(\"trailing.jsonl\")).unwrap(),\n            \"{\\\"line\\\":1}\\n\",\n            \"trailing content preserved\"\n        );\n    }\n\n    /// Scenario 2b: `@` has trailing writes and the remote advanced\n    /// while the session was offline. jj's fetch auto-ff's main but\n    /// leaves `@` behind; sync must then rebase `@` onto the new main\n    /// so the session tail doesn't end up orphaned. Content is\n    /// preserved across the rebase.\n    #[test]\n    fn sync_rebases_trailing_at_when_main_moves() {\n        let fx = Fixture::new(\"trailing-rebase\");\n        let remote_claude = fx.base.join(\"remote-claude.git\");\n        let remote_head = push_from_clone(\n            &fx.base,\n            &remote_claude,\n            \"claude2\",\n            \"remote.md\",\n            \"remote\\n\",\n            \"feat: remote-added\",\n        );\n        // Trailing writes on @\n        fs::write(fx.claude.join(\"trailing.jsonl\"), \"{\\\"line\\\":2}\\n\")\n            .expect(\"write trailing file\");\n        sync_repos(&fx.repos(), &apply_args()).expect(\"sync should succeed\");\n        assert_eq!(\n            cid(&fx.claude, \"main\"),\n            remote_head,\n            \"local main should match remote after auto-ff\"\n        );\n        // @ should now be a descendant of main.\n        let on_main = jj(\n            &fx.claude,\n            &[\n                \"log\",\n                \"-r\",\n                \"main::@\",\n                \"--no-graph\",\n                \"-T\",\n                r#\"commit_id.short(12) ++ \"\\n\"\"#,\n            ],\n        );\n        assert!(\n            !on_main.trim().is_empty(),\n            \"@ should be reachable from main after ensure_at_on_main\"\n        );\n        // Trailing content preserved on disk.\n        assert_eq!(\n            fs::read_to_string(fx.claude.join(\"trailing.jsonl\")).unwrap(),\n            \"{\\\"line\\\":2}\\n\",\n            \"trailing content preserved across @ rebase\"\n        );\n    }\n\n    /// Scenario 2c: `@` has trailing writes and local+remote both\n    /// modify the same file differently. Rebase produces conflicts;\n    /// sync fails and op-restores both repos. The trailing content\n    /// must survive the revert — jj documents that op restore rolls\n    /// back operation history without clobbering the working copy.\n    #[test]\n    fn sync_conflict_preserves_trailing_at_on_revert() {\n        let fx = Fixture::new(\"trailing-conflict\");\n        let remote_claude = fx.base.join(\"remote-claude.git\");\n        push_from_clone(\n            &fx.base,\n            &remote_claude,\n            \"claude2\",\n            \"shared.txt\",\n            \"remote-version\\n\",\n            \"feat: remote shared\",\n        );\n        // Local commit on main with conflicting content\n        add_local_commit(\n            &fx.claude,\n            \"shared.txt\",\n            \"local-version\\n\",\n            \"feat: local shared (conflicting)\",\n        );\n        // Trailing writes on new @\n        fs::write(fx.claude.join(\"trailing.jsonl\"), \"{\\\"line\\\":3}\\n\")\n            .expect(\"write trailing file\");\n\n        let pre_main = cid(&fx.claude, \"main\");\n        let pre_remote = cid(&fx.claude, \"main@origin\");\n\n        let err = sync_repos(&fx.repos(), &apply_args())\n            .unwrap_err()\n            .to_string();\n        assert!(\n            err.contains(\"conflicts\"),\n            \"expected conflict error, got: {err}\"\n        );\n\n        // Post-revert: main and main@origin back to pre-sync.\n        assert_eq!(cid(&fx.claude, \"main\"), pre_main, \"main reverted\");\n        assert_eq!(\n            cid(&fx.claude, \"main@origin\"),\n            pre_remote,\n            \"main@origin reverted\"\n        );\n        // No conflicts remain.\n        let conflicts = jj(\n            &fx.claude,\n            &[\n                \"log\",\n                \"-r\",\n                \"conflicts()\",\n                \"--no-graph\",\n                \"-T\",\n                r#\"commit_id ++ \"\\n\"\"#,\n            ],\n        );\n        assert!(\n            conflicts.trim().is_empty(),\n            \"no conflicts should remain after revert\"\n        );\n        // Trailing content preserved on disk.\n        assert_eq!(\n            fs::read_to_string(fx.claude.join(\"trailing.jsonl\")).unwrap(),\n            \"{\\\"line\\\":3}\\n\",\n            \"trailing content preserved across op restore\"\n        );\n    }"
}

Per repo:\\n  \\\n          - up-to-date        nothing to do\\n  \\\n          - behind            fast-forward bookmark to remote\\n  \\\n          - ahead             nothing to sync (local has unpushed work)\\n  \\\n          - diverged          rebase local onto remote; fail on conflicts\\n  \\\n          - no remote         bookmark has no @<remote> counterpart; skip\\n\\n\\\n        After the bookmark action, `@` is rebased onto the (possibly\\n\\\n        advanced) bookmark if it isn't already a descendant, so trailing\\n\\\n        working-copy writes (e.g. `.claude`'s `/exit` tail) don't end up\\n\\\n        orphaned on a stale branch.\\n\\n\\\n        On any failure, both repos are reverted to their starting state\\n\\\n        via `jj op restore`. {
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/README.md",
  "old_string": "Pre-flight requires a clean working-copy commit (`@` empty — no diff\nfrom `@-`) in every repo. This rules out running `sync` mid-session:\nthe `.claude` repo is non-empty during an active session, and aborting\nsync's revert path would undo that session work.\n\nOn any failure — conflicted rebase, subprocess error, anything — `sync`\nrestores every repo to its starting state via `jj op restore`. Either\nevery repo advances or none do.",
  "new_string": "After the bookmark action above, `sync` also rebases `@` onto the\n(possibly advanced) bookmark when `@` isn't already a descendant —\nwithout this step, `jj git fetch`'s auto-fast-forward would leave `@`\ndangling off the pre-fetch bookmark commit. This matters for `.claude`,\nwhere `/exit`'s trailing session writes always sit on `@`.\n\nOn any failure — conflicted rebase, subprocess error, anything — `sync`\nrestores every repo to its starting state via `jj op restore`. Either\nevery repo advances or none do. Working-copy files are preserved\nacross the revert: jj rewinds the operation log but leaves disk\ncontent untouched, and any conflicted commits introduced by the failed\nrebase are abandoned on the way back."
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/notes/chores-04.md",
  "old_string": "### Pre-flight: `@` must be empty\n\n`sync` refuses to run if either repo's working-copy commit (`@`) is\nnon-empty. \"Empty\" means no diff from parent (`@ & empty()` revset).\nThat rules out both:\n- The app repo with uncommitted user edits snapshotted into `@`.\n- The `.claude` repo during an active bot session (session jsonl\n  writes make `@` non-empty until finalize squashes them).\n\nThe revert path is `jj op restore <pre-sync-op>`, which rolls back\noperation state across both repos. If `@` had uncommitted work, the\nrevert would undo it — so we refuse up front rather than risk losing\nsession writes.",
  "new_string": "### No pre-flight: trust `jj op restore` + explicit `@` rebase\n\nThe first draft of `sync` required `@` to be empty (`@ & empty()`\nrevset) in every repo before running, on the theory that the revert\npath (`jj op restore`) would clobber uncommitted work. That ruled out\nthe most common real-world case — `.claude`'s `@` is always non-empty\nafter `/exit` writes its tail.\n\nTwo empirical findings from fixture testing changed the design:\n\n1. **`jj op restore` preserves working-copy content across conflicted\n   rebases.** Setup: local commit modifying `shared.txt`, `@` above\n   with `trailing.jsonl`, remote with a conflicting `shared.txt`.\n   Fetch + rebase → 2 conflicted commits. `jj op restore <pre-sync>`\n   → `main` and `main@origin` back to their pre-sync ids, `@` back to\n   the *same* commit id it had before the sync (not a new revert\n   commit), `conflicts()` empty, both files (`trailing.jsonl` and\n   `shared.txt`) on disk with their original content. jj's own output\n   confirms it: \"Existing conflicts were resolved or abandoned from 2\n   commits.\"\n\n2. **`jj git fetch`'s auto-fast-forward leaves `@` behind.** When a\n   tracked local bookmark is a strict ancestor of the freshly fetched\n   remote, jj advances the bookmark — but `@`'s parent pointer stays\n   on the *old* bookmark commit. `@` is now on a dangling branch,\n   which means any subsequent `jj new`, commit, or finalize would\n   build on stale history. Easy to reproduce, easy to miss.\n\nSo the design became:\n- **No pre-flight check** — the revert path preserves files.\n- **After the bookmark action, always call `ensure_at_on_main(repo,\n  bookmark)`.** If `main::@` is empty (i.e., `@` isn't reachable from\n  the bookmark), run `jj rebase -b @ -d <bookmark>` to pull `@` and\n  any commits between the old parent and `@` forward. Then check\n  `conflicts()` — a conflicted rebase trips the outer revert the same\n  way a conflicted `act_on_state` rebase does.\n- **Revert preserves trailing writes.** An integration test\n  (`sync_conflict_preserves_trailing_at_on_revert`) pins this\n  behavior so a future jj version that changed it would fail CI."
}
```

Fixtures clean themselves up\nvia a `Drop` impl so panicking tests still remove their tempdirs.\n\nTests require `jj` in `PATH` at test time — new property for this\nrepo, but `sync` itself shells out to `jj` so the tests can't avoid\nthe dependency.\n\n- **`sync_up_to_date`** — fresh fixture, `--no-dry-run`; assert both\n  repos' `main` commit ids unchanged.\n- **`sync_preflight_rejects_dirty_at`** — write a file into `work/`\n  so `@` becomes non-empty on next snapshot; assert error string\n  contains \"working-copy commit (@) is not empty\".\n- **`sync_ahead_is_noop`** — add a local commit on `main`,\n  `--no-dry-run`; assert `main` commit id unchanged after sync.\n- **`sync_diverged_rebases`** — a second clone pushes a commit to\n  `remote-code.git`; local adds a different commit on `main`;\n  `--no-dry-run` triggers the rebase path. Asserts:\n  - `main@origin` advanced to the pushed remote commit.\n  - Local `main` is a different id than before (rebased).\n  - Remote head is an ancestor of local `main`.\n  - `conflicts()` revset is empty.\n- **`sync_diverged_conflict_reverts`** — both sides write different\n  contents to the same path; rebase produces conflicts; sync errors\n  with \"rebase produced conflicts\"; assert both `main` and\n  `main@origin` are back at their pre-sync ids and `conflicts()`\n  revset is empty (so the revert was clean across fetch + rebase).\n\nTotal run time ~1s for the five-test module on a local workstation.",
  "new_string": "### Integration tests (`src/sync.rs::integration_tests`)\n\nFive integration tests drive real dual-repo fixtures (built via\n`crate::test_fixture::test_fixture`, seeded optionally from the\n`vc-template-x1` template pair) under unique tempdirs, then exercise\neach scenario with `jj` subprocess calls and assert on the resulting\nstate. Fixtures clean themselves up via a `Drop` impl so panicking\ntests still remove their tempdirs.\n\nTests require `jj` in `PATH` at test time — new property for this\nrepo, but `sync` itself shells out to `jj` so the tests can't avoid\nthe dependency.\n\n- **`sync_up_to_date`** — fresh fixture, `--no-dry-run`; assert both\n  repos' `main` commit ids unchanged.\n- **`sync_tolerates_trailing_at_up_to_date`** — write a file into\n  `.claude/` so `@` snapshots non-empty; assert main is stable, `@`\n  stays reachable from main (`main::@` non-empty), and the trailing\n  file content is preserved. Pins the \"no pre-flight\" rule.\n- **`sync_rebases_trailing_at_when_main_moves`** — remote advances\n  via a second clone; local `@` has trailing jsonl content; sync\n  (`--no-dry-run`) runs fetch + auto-ff + `ensure_at_on_main`. Asserts\n  local `main` matches the pushed remote head, `@` is now reachable\n  from main (not orphaned on the pre-fetch commit), and trailing\n  content is preserved on disk.\n- **`sync_ahead_is_noop`** — add a local commit on `main`,\n  `--no-dry-run`; assert `main` commit id unchanged after sync.\n- **`sync_diverged_rebases`** — a second clone pushes a commit;\n  local adds a different commit on `main`; `--no-dry-run` triggers\n  the rebase path. Asserts `main@origin` advanced, local `main` is\n  rebased to a new id with the remote head as ancestor, and\n  `conflicts()` is empty.\n- **`sync_conflict_preserves_trailing_at_on_revert`** — both sides\n  write different contents to the same path; `@` also has trailing\n  jsonl writes. Rebase produces conflicts; sync errors with\n  \"conflicts\"; the outer revert restores `main`, `main@origin`, and\n  `@` to their pre-sync state. Asserts `conflicts()` is empty and\n  the trailing jsonl content is intact on disk. Pins jj's\n  op-restore-preserves-working-copy behavior.\n\nTotal run time ~1s for the seven-test module on a local workstation."
}

741	
742	2. **`jj git fetch`'s auto-fast-forward leaves `@` behind.** When a
743	   tracked local bookmark is a strict ancestor of the freshly fetched
744	   remote, jj advances the bookmark — but `@`'s parent pointer stays
745	   on the *old* bookmark commit. `@` is now on a dangling branch,
746	   which means any subsequent `jj new`, commit, or finalize would
747	   build on stale history. Easy to reproduce, easy to miss.
748	
749	So the design became:
750	- **No pre-flight check** — the revert path preserves files.
751	- **After the bookmark action, always call `ensure_at_on_main(repo,
752	  bookmark)`.** If `main::@` is empty (i.e., `@` isn't reachable from
753	  the bookmark), run `jj rebase -b @ -d <bookmark>` to pull `@` and
754	  any commits between the old parent and `@` forward. Then check
755	  `conflicts()` — a conflicted rebase trips the outer revert the same
756	  way a conflicted `act_on_state` rebase does.
757	- **Revert preserves trailing writes.** An integration test
758	  (`sync_conflict_preserves_trailing_at_on_revert`) pins this
759	  behavior so a future jj version that changed it would fail CI.
760	
761	### States and actions
762	
763	| State | Detection | `--no-dry-run` action |
764	|------|----------|-----------------------|
765	| `UpToDate` | `bookmarks(exact:<b>) == {<b>@<remote>}` | none |
766	| `Behind` | single local head is strict ancestor of remote | `jj bookmark set <b> -r <b>@<remote>` |
767	| `Ahead` | single local head is strict descendant of remote | none (push is separate) |
768	| `Diverged` | conflicted bookmark (multiple heads) *or* neither is ancestor | `jj rebase -b <local-only-head> -d <b>@<remote>`, then check `conflicts()` |
769	| `NoRemote` | `<b>@<remote>` does not resolve | none — skip |
770	
771	Note: the `Behind` branch is largely redundant with jj's own
772	fetch-auto-fast-forward behavior (tracked bookmarks whose local is a
773	strict ancestor auto-advance on fetch), so in practice the classify
774	result post-fetch is usually `UpToDate`. `Behind` is kept for untracked
775	bookmarks and edge configs where auto-advance is disabled.
776	
777	### `bookmarks(exact:<b>)` for conflicted detection
778	
779	When `jj git fetch` produces a divergence, the local bookmark becomes
780	conflicted — it points at *both* its old local tip and the
781	freshly-fetched remote tip. The bare `main` revset then errors with
782	`Name \`main\` is conflicted`. `bookmarks(exact:main)` returns all
783	heads instead, so `classify` can see the multi-head case and classify
784	it as `Diverged` directly. The rebase picks the non-remote head from
```

> AGENT

All checks pass (167 tests). Here are the two squash commands to amend the 0.35.0 commits in place — `-m` sets the updated description, `jj squash` folds the working-copy changes into `@-`:

**App repo:**
```
jj squash -R . \
-m "feat: add sync subcommand (0.35.0)" \
-m "Fetch and sync both repos (\`.\` and \`.claude\`) to their remotes
in a single command. Default is dry-run — \`--no-dry-run\` to act.

Per repo, classify local bookmark vs \`<b>@<remote>\`:
  up-to-date | behind (ff) | ahead | diverged (rebase) | no remote

After the bookmark action, \`sync\` also rebases \`@\` onto the
(possibly advanced) bookmark when \`@\` isn't already a descendant.
Without this, jj's fetch auto-ff would leave \`@\` dangling off the
pre-fetch commit, orphaning \`.claude\`'s /exit tail on a stale branch.

On any failure — conflicted rebase, subprocess error, anything — both
repos are reverted via \`jj op restore\`. Verified empirically that jj
preserves working-copy files and abandons conflicted commits during
the revert, so trailing session writes survive a failed sync intact.

The diverged case handles jj's conflicted-bookmark shape directly:
after a diverged fetch, \`main\` has two heads; \`bookmarks(exact:main)\`
enumerates them and the non-remote head becomes the rebase source.

Testability: \`sync()\` is a thin CLI wrapper over \`sync_repos(repos,
args)\`, so integration tests pass absolute tempdir paths and avoid
\`chdir\` races under parallel \`cargo test\`. Five integration tests
cover up-to-date, trailing-@ up-to-date, trailing-@ with remote
advance (\`ensure_at_on_main\`), ahead, diverged clean rebase, and
trailing-@ with conflict revert. Plus two arg-parse unit tests.
Requires \`jj\` in \`PATH\` at test time.

ochid: /.claude/qpksmolwqtqw"
```

**`.claude` repo:**
```
jj squash -R .claude \
-m "feat: add sync subcommand (0.35.0)" \
-m "Session: initial sync required an empty \`@\` pre-flight. During
manual testing the user pointed out that /exit always leaves trailing
jsonl writes on \`.claude\`'s \`@\`, so the common case would always
fail. Verified empirically that jj op restore preserves working-copy
content across conflicted rebases, and that jj's fetch auto-ff leaves
\`@\` behind. Redesigned sync to drop the pre-flight and add an
\`ensure_at_on_main\` step that rebases \`@\` forward after the
bookmark action. Integration tests now pin both the trailing-@ rebase
path and the op-restore-preserves-WC behavior.

Saved a feedback memory: every fn/method in vc-x1 source needs a
\`///\` doc comment — matches the existing project style.

ochid: /ypnqupwospsn"
```

Approve to execute both? (Chids are stable across rewrites — the ochid trailers stay valid.)
